1	# 217 — Concat box with per-input variadic ports and auto-grow/shrink
2	
3	## Status
4	complete (Part A, B); reopened for Part C — spec-driven variadic
5	correctness, blocked on issue 231
6	
7	## Part C — reject `var` on a non-variadic parameter
8	
9	The editor's `var` toggle is a user-asserted flag today. If the
10	underlying function signature doesn't accept varargs, the editor
11	still lets the user mark a parameter variadic; at runtime the
12	extra positional args are silently swallowed (Lua / Bash drop
13	unreferenced trailing args; C's fixed-signature wrapper either
14	ignores them or errors depending on how `n_inputs` is consumed).
15	
16	This is the silent-error class — a sibling of the missing-input
17	case issue 230 closed off. The editor knows enough about the
18	function (the file browser parsed its signature when the user
19	picked it) to refuse the `var` toggle on a non-variadic
20	parameter, but the parser today lives inside the editor's
21	JavaScript and hard-codes the per-language rules — so the
22	detection's natural home is wherever the per-language parser
23	moves to next. That destination is issue 231 (move signature
24	parsers into `langs/<name>/parser.js`).
25	
26	### Behavior after 231 lands
27	
28	The `parse_function_signature` ES-module export from each
29	language's `parser.js` already returns a `variadic_inputs: [...]`
30	array — names of parameters that the language considers
31	variadic. The inspector consumes that list at render time:
32	
33	- A port whose name is in `variadic_inputs` → the `var` button
34	  renders as today (toggleable; the user can collapse the group
35	  if they want).
36	- A port whose name is NOT in `variadic_inputs` → the `var`
37	  button is hidden (or disabled with a tooltip explaining why),
38	  so the user cannot wire a five-slot variadic group into a
39	  function that only accepts one positional arg.
40	- Pre-existing maps that already carry a non-variadic-in-source
41	  variadic group: validator surfaces them as an error when the
42	  Compile button (issue 309) runs, same shape as 230's binding
43	  errors. The user fixes by collapsing the group.
44	
45	### Implementation steps (post-231)
46	
47	1. `assets/js/004-inspector.js::mk_port_display` — when the
48	   current box is a `call` box with a `ref`, ask the parser
49	   module for the function's signature (cache per-box) and
50	   decide whether to render the `var` button per the rules
51	   above.
52	2. New graph-level check in `src/001-schema.lua` (or wherever
53	   231 puts the per-language signature lookup): for every box
54	   in `variadic_inputs`, the parsed signature must agree.
55	   Mismatches → errors in the same list `check_input_bindings`
56	   uses.
57	3. CLI mirror in `src/002-validate-map.lua` so the
58	   command-line validator catches drift too.
59	
60	### Why it sits here and not in 231
61	
62	231's scope is one mechanical move — pull `parse_lua` /
63	`parse_bash` out of the file browser into per-language ES
64	modules. Adding the variadic-correctness consequence to 231
65	would conflate the relocation with new behavior. Keeping the
66	behavior on 217 — the issue that introduced the variadic
67	mechanic — means a reader following the variadic story finds
68	both the original feature and the eventual correctness check
69	in the same file. 231 stays focused on relocation; 217 records
70	the consequence of relocation.
71	
72	---
73	
74	## Implementation notes (Part A, B — shipped)
75	
76	**Part A** — `libs/text.lua` ships `M.concat(sep, ...)` and
77	`M.split(text, sep)`. Concat is variadic to match the editor's
78	variadic-input model. Split returns a Lua array; the driver shim
79	JSON-encodes for the single output wire. The accompanying
80	`libs/text.lua.info.md` documents both functions.
81	
82	**Part B** — Variadic UI lives in `assets/js/004-inspector.js`:
83	
84	- Helpers (`parse_variadic_name`, `is_variadic_slot`,
85	  `variadic_slots_for`, `last_variadic_index`) parse and query the
86	  `<base>_<index>` naming convention for variadic slots.
87	- `make_variadic(port_name)` toggles a non-variadic port into a
88	  one-slot variadic group (rename `text` → `text_0`, add base to
89	  `box.variadic_inputs`, update connection records on this box and
90	  every upstream box).
91	- `unmake_variadic(base)` collapses a group back to a single port
92	  (drop higher slots and their wires, rename slot 0 back to base).
93	- `add_variadic_slot(base)` appends `<base>_<last+1>`. Used by the
94	  auto-grow path; also reachable directly when needed.
95	- `remove_variadic_slot(slot_name)` drops a single slot and renames
96	  higher slots down by one, updating connections atomically per
97	  slot.
98	- `auto_grow_after_set(box, slot_name)` is the public-from-Inspector
99	  helper. If `slot_name` is the last slot of its variadic group, it
100	  appends a new empty slot. Idempotent — calling again on the same
101	  slot is a no-op once the slot is no longer last.
102	
103	The wire-create path (`006-wires.js::create_connection`) calls
104	`Inspector.auto_grow_after_set(dst_box, to_input)` after a
105	successful PUT. The inspector's per-port value-input handler also
106	calls it when a non-empty literal value is set on a slot. Together
107	these cover both growth triggers — wire-connect and value-set.
108	
109	Per-port UI (`mk_port_display`):
110	
111	- Plain port → `var` toggle (turn variadic on).
112	- First slot of a variadic group → `var ×` toggle (collapse back).
113	- Non-first variadic slot → `×` (remove this slot only; higher
114	  slots rename down).
115	- No `+` button — slots auto-grow. Wire-disconnect and
116	  value-clear do NOT shrink (only `×` removes structure).
117	
118	Schema accepts `variadic_inputs` as an optional array of strings
119	(`src/001-schema.lua`).
120	
121	Comparator toggle (`004-inspector.js::cmp_btn.onclick`) now severs
122	all outgoing wires on either toggle direction, fixing the prior
123	behavior where lt/eq/gt wires from a previous comparator state
124	silently re-appeared when the user re-enabled it.
125	
126	## Deliberate omissions / follow-ups
127	
128	- **Auto-shrink on wire-disconnect** is intentionally not done —
129	  the user explicitly chose "explicit `×` is the only way to shrink"
130	  so transient editing doesn't lose structure.
131	- **Editable port names** and **value editing on the canvas
132	  itself** belong in issue 224 — bigger redesign than 217 covers.
133	- **Future ideas** (language-spec-driven variadic detection,
134	  alternative variadic shapes for keyword args / streams /
135	  discriminated unions) are captured below.
136	
137	## Current behavior
138	No standard concat box exists. No mechanism exists for a box to have a variable
139	number of input wires on a single logical port.
140	
141	## Intended behavior
142	
143	### Part A — text library box
144	`libs/text.lua` exposes two functions:
145	
146	`M.concat(sep, ...)` — join N strings:
147	- `sep` — separator string (e.g. `"\n\n"`)
148	- `...` — any number of text values to join (variadic)
149	- returns a single `result` port
150	
151	The function is variadic so its `text` input can expand to any count without
152	modifying the source file.
153	
154	`M.split(text, sep)` — split one string into parts:
155	- `text` — the input string
156	- `sep` — the separator to split on (plain string, not a pattern)
157	- returns a single `result` port containing a JSON-encoded array of the parts
158	
159	Output ports are named at wire-design time, so a variable number of runtime
160	parts cannot map to separate wires. A JSON array on one port is the only
161	consistent design — the downstream box decodes it.
162	
163	Note: one output wire per box is a deliberate design constraint, not a missing
164	feature. Positional multi-output was explicitly rejected as fragile (multiple
165	return statements with the same tuple width are indistinguishable). The
166	comparator (lt/eq/gt branching) is the only multi-path mechanism. Whatever the
167	function returns goes down the single output wire as one value — arrays or JSON
168	objects if multiple values need to travel together.
169	
170	### Part B — per-input variadic flag
171	
172	Variadic is a property of a specific input port, not the box as a whole. A box
173	could have `sep` (fixed) and `text` (variadic). The inspector shows a toggle on
174	each input to mark it variadic.
175	
176	#### Data model
177	
178	The box JSON gains a `variadic_inputs` set (array of base names):
179	```json
180	{
181	  "inputs": ["sep", "text_0", "text_1", "text_2"],
182	  "variadic_inputs": ["text"]
183	}
184	```
185	
186	Slots of a variadic input are stored as `<base>_<index>` in the `inputs` array.
187	The executor already passes all inputs positionally — no executor changes needed.
188	Connections reference slots by their indexed name (`"text_0"`, `"text_1"`, etc.).
189	
190	The parser (Lua: detects `...`, bash: detects `$@` after shift) can auto-populate
191	`variadic_inputs` when a file is selected via the file browser. For other languages
192	(C, etc.) the user sets the toggle manually in the inspector — the executor contract
193	already supports N positional args via `arg_count`, so C functions reading `argv[]`
194	in a loop work without any changes.
195	
196	#### Auto-grow
197	When a wire is connected to the LAST slot of a variadic input, the editor appends
198	a new empty slot (`text_N+1`) to `box.inputs` and saves the box. The new slot
199	appears immediately as an open port.
200	
201	#### Auto-shrink
202	When a wire is disconnected from any slot of a variadic input:
203	1. Remove that slot from `box.inputs`.
204	2. Rename all subsequent slots down by one (`text_2` → `text_1`, etc.).
205	3. Update every connection that referenced a renamed slot:
206	   - `to_input` in the connection record on this box
207	   - The corresponding side on the source box (connections are stored in both
208	     endpoint files; both must be updated atomically).
209	4. Keep at least one slot for the variadic input (never shrink below `text_0`).
210	
211	Middle-port removal is handled by the same rename-and-compact step: remove the cut
212	slot, shift all higher-indexed slots down, update their wires. The user always sees
213	a dense, gap-free list of ports.
214	
215	## Open questions before implementing Part B
216	- Rename atomicity: the server's `PUT /maps/:name/boxes/:id` writes one box at a
217	  time. Updating the connection in the source box requires a second PUT. These are
218	  two separate writes — consider whether a partial failure leaves the map in an
219	  inconsistent state, and whether a `PUT /maps/:name/connections` batch endpoint
220	  is worth adding first.
221	- Should the file browser parser set `variadic_inputs` automatically when it
222	  detects `...` in a Lua signature, and if so, which argument specifically?
223	
224	  -- nah let's just make the user set the variadic property manually. Unless we can put it in the language driver
225	     somehow? but I don't think that's really possible in the design. Thoughts?
226	
227	## Suggested implementation sequence
228	1. Part A: implement `libs/text.lua` with `M.concat(sep, ...)`.
229	2. Part B schema: add `variadic_inputs` to box schema and validator.
230	3. Part B editor: auto-grow on wire connect; auto-shrink + rename on wire disconnect.
231	4. Part B inspector: variadic toggle per input port.
232	5. Part B parser: auto-detect `...` in Lua and set `variadic_inputs`.
233	
234	## Future development ideas
235	
236	### Language-spec-driven variadic detection
237	
238	Currently the editor uses hard-coded heuristics per language to find
239	variadic indicators in a function signature (Lua: literal `...`, Bash:
240	`$@` after `shift`). The cleaner architecture mirrors the phase 3
241	language-spec model (issue 303): each language declares its own way
242	to recognize and surface variadic parameters, and the file-browser
243	parser asks the language spec rather than embedding the rules.
244	
245	Concretely, the editor side of a language spec
246	(`langs/<name>/lexer.js` or a sibling `parser.js`) could expose:
247	
248	```js
249	// Returns { name, inputs: ["sep", "text"], variadic_inputs: ["text"] }
250	// for `function M.concat(sep, ...)` in Lua, or
251	// `concat() { local sep="$1"; shift; ... "$@" }` in Bash.
252	function parse_function_signature(source, fn_name) { ... }
253	```
254	
255	The editor still calls the same on-select handler; the language spec
256	is responsible for deciding how to translate native variadic syntax
257	into the editor's `(inputs, variadic_inputs)` model.
258	
259	### Variadic shapes that don't map cleanly to an array
260	
261	The current model assumes variadic = an ordered series of positional
262	slots. That fits Lua `...`, C `va_list`, Bash `"$@"`, JavaScript rest
263	params, etc. It does not fit:
264	
265	- **Keyword arguments** (Python `**kwargs`, Ruby keyword splats) —
266	  these are key/value pairs, not positional. A box would need
267	  named-slot pairs the user labels per slot, not auto-numbered
268	  `_0` / `_1` / `_2`.
269	- **Tagged unions / discriminated lists** (Rust enum lists, Haskell
270	  variadic via heterogeneous lists) — each entry has a type tag in
271	  addition to a value. Two-field slots, or per-slot type metadata.
272	- **Streaming inputs** (generators, channels, iterators) — the
273	  consumer doesn't know in advance how many values arrive. This is
274	  closer to issue 213's queued-input model than to variadic.
275	
276	A future revision of the editor's variadic UI could let language
277	specs choose between several "variadic shapes":
278	
279	| shape           | slot model                          | example use      |
280	|-----------------|-------------------------------------|------------------|
281	| `positional`    | `<base>_<index>` ordered (current)  | Lua `...`        |
282	| `keyed`         | per-slot `(key, value)` pairs       | Python `**kwargs`|
283	| `tagged`        | per-slot `(tag, value)` pairs       | discriminated unions |
284	| `stream`        | one slot, queued (issue 213)        | iterators        |
285	
286	Each shape would have its own auto-grow rule and inspector controls.
287	The language spec declares which shape applies to a given variadic
288	parameter, and the editor renders accordingly.
289	
290	This is speculative — the positional shape covers every shipped
291	language so far. Capturing it here so the design door stays open.
292	
293	## Relevant files
294	- `libs/ollama.lua` — reference pattern for a library box
295	- `assets/js/006-wires.js` — wire connect/disconnect events
296	- `assets/js/004-inspector.js` — input port list, toggle rendering
297	- `src/001-schema.lua` — box validator (`variadic_inputs` field)
298	- `src/005-http-server.lua` — PUT box endpoint (rename propagation)
299	- `issues/208-port-literal-values.md` — `sep` will likely use literal port values
300	
```

```
1	// langs/lua/parser.js — Lua signature parser for the editor's file
2	// browser. Issue 231 moved this code out of assets/js/007-filebrowser.js
3	// so per-language knowledge lives next to the rest of each language's
4	// pieces (spec.c, Makefile, lexer.js).
5	//
6	// The contract is one ES-module export, `parse_functions(content)`,
7	// returning an array of records describing every public function the
8	// browser should offer. The file browser does the rest — listing,
9	// click-to-pick, port population.
10	
11	// {{{ lua_fn_end
12	// Returns the index of the closing 'end' / 'until' that matches the
13	// function body starting at body_start. Tracks nesting depth by
14	// counting block openers and closers. Does not attempt to skip string
15	// literals — good enough for well-formatted source.
16	function lua_fn_end(content, body_start) {
17	  const tok = /\b(function|if|while|for|repeat|do|end|until)\b/g;
18	  tok.lastIndex = body_start;
19	  let depth = 1;
20	  let m;
21	  while ((m = tok.exec(content)) !== null) {
22	    const kw = m[1];
23	    if (kw === 'end' || kw === 'until') {
24	      depth--;
25	      if (depth === 0) return m.index;
26	    } else {
27	      depth++;
28	    }
29	  }
30	  return content.length;
31	}
32	// }}}
33	
34	// {{{ parse_returns
35	// Scans a function body for `return` statements; returns an array of
36	// output names. Bare identifiers in the return list become port names;
37	// any complex expression collapses to a single "result" port.
38	function parse_returns(body) {
39	  const re    = /\breturn\b\s*([^\n]+)/g;
40	  const names = new Set();
41	  let m;
42	  while ((m = re.exec(body)) !== null) {
43	    const expr = m[1].replace(/--.*$/, '').trim();
44	    if (!expr) continue;
45	    if (/["']|\.\./.test(expr)) { names.add('result'); continue; }
46	    const parts = expr.split(',');
47	    parts.forEach(part => {
48	      part = part.trim();
49	      if (/^[a-zA-Z_]\w*$/.test(part) &&
50	          !['nil','true','false','not','and','or','end'].includes(part)) {
51	        names.add(part);
52	      } else if (parts.length === 1 && part) {
53	        names.add('result');
54	      }
55	    });
56	  }
57	  return [...names];
58	}
59	// }}}
60	
61	// {{{ parse_functions
62	// Returns [{name, inputs, outputs, variadic_tail}] for every
63	// `function M.<name>(<params>)` declared in the file.
64	//
65	// The `variadic_tail` field is the addition from issue 217 part C
66	// (reopened): true when the parameter list ends with `...`, meaning
67	// the function accepts more positional args than its declared params.
68	// The trailing `...` is stripped from `inputs` so the editor's port
69	// list reflects only the named parameters; the editor uses
70	// `variadic_tail` to decide whether marking a port variadic is
71	// actually supported by the source.
72	export function parse_functions(content) {
73	  const fns   = [];
74	  const fn_re = /^function\s+M\.(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)/mg;
75	  let m;
76	  while ((m = fn_re.exec(content)) !== null) {
77	    const body_start = m.index + m[0].length;
78	    const body_end   = lua_fn_end(content, body_start);
79	    const body       = content.slice(body_start, body_end);
80	    const raw_params = m[2].trim()
81	      ? m[2].split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean)
82	      : [];
83	    const variadic_tail = raw_params[raw_params.length - 1] === '...';
84	    const inputs        = variadic_tail ? raw_params.slice(0, -1) : raw_params;
85	    const outputs       = parse_returns(body);
86	    fns.push({ name: m[1], inputs, outputs, variadic_tail });
87	  }
88	  return fns;
89	}
90	// }}}
91	
```

1	// langs/bash/parser.js — Bash signature parser for the editor's file
2	// browser. Issue 231 moved this code out of assets/js/007-filebrowser.js
3	// so per-language knowledge lives next to the rest of each language's
4	// pieces (spec.c, Makefile, lexer.js).
5	
6	// {{{ parse_inputs
7	// Counts positional args a function references and names them after
8	// any `local var="$N"` assignments. Falls back to generic `argN` when
9	// no local binding is found. Returns an array sized to the highest
10	// `$N` the function touches.
11	function parse_inputs(body) {
12	  const params = {};
13	  const local_re = /local\s+(\w+)\s*=\s*["']?\$\{?(\d+)\}?["']?/g;
14	  let m;
15	  while ((m = local_re.exec(body)) !== null) {
16	    params[parseInt(m[2])] = m[1];
17	  }
18	  if (Object.keys(params).length > 0) {
19	    const max = Math.max(...Object.keys(params).map(Number));
20	    return Array.from({ length: max }, (_, i) => params[i + 1] || `arg${i + 1}`);
21	  }
22	  const seen = new Set();
23	  const pos_re = /\$\{?(\d+)\}?/g;
24	  while ((m = pos_re.exec(body)) !== null) {
25	    const n = parseInt(m[1]);
26	    if (n > 0) seen.add(n);
27	  }
28	  if (seen.size > 0) {
29	    const max = Math.max(...seen);
30	    return Array.from({ length: max }, (_, i) => `arg${i + 1}`);
31	  }
32	  return [];
33	}
34	// }}}
35	
36	// {{{ parse_functions
37	// Returns [{name, inputs, outputs, variadic_tail}] for every shell
38	// function declared in the file.
39	//
40	// Output is always a single named port `output` — Bash functions emit
41	// one value via stdout (issue 218's single-output rule).
42	//
43	// `variadic_tail` is true when the function references `"$@"` or
44	// bare `$@` after the named-positional region. Issue 217 part C uses
45	// this to gate the editor's `var` toggle: a function that doesn't
46	// touch `$@` shouldn't accept variadic input from the editor.
47	export function parse_functions(content) {
48	  const fns  = [];
49	  const re   = /^(\w+)\s*\(\s*\)\s*\{/mg;
50	  const skip = new Set(['if','while','for','case','do','done','fi','then','else']);
51	  let m;
52	  while ((m = re.exec(content)) !== null) {
53	    const name = m[1];
54	    if (skip.has(name)) continue;
55	    const body_start = m.index + m[0].length;
56	    const close      = content.indexOf('\n}', body_start);
57	    const body       = content.slice(body_start, close > -1 ? close : body_start + 800);
58	    const inputs     = parse_inputs(body);
59	    const variadic_tail = /\$@|\$\{@\}|"\$@"/.test(body);
60	    fns.push({ name, inputs, outputs: ['output'], variadic_tail });
61	  }
62	  return fns;
63	}
64	// }}}
65	
```

1	#!/bin/bash
2	# scripts/run-tests.sh — phase 3 integration tests.
3	#
4	# Runs every fixture map under tests/maps/ through `soramech-pool`
5	# and asserts that the captured outputs match a hardcoded
6	# expectation per map. The point is to catch regressions
7	# end-to-end — past the spec interface, past the dispatch action,
8	# past JSONL writing — rather than via the in-process unit tests.
9	#
10	# Per the project convention: scripts run from any directory via a
11	# hard-coded ${DIR} path with an override argument; all paths are
12	# relative to ${DIR}.
13	
14	set -u
15	
16	DIR="/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech"
17	if [[ $# -ge 1 ]]; then
18	    DIR="$1"
19	fi
20	
21	cd "$DIR"
22	
23	pass=0
24	fail=0
25	failures=()
26	
27	# {{{ check_map() — run one fixture, verify expected substrings
28	check_map() {
29	    local map="$1"
30	    shift
31	    local expected=("$@")
32	
33	    printf "  %-44s " "$map"
34	
35	    local output
36	    output=$("$DIR/soramech-pool" "$DIR/tests/maps/$map" 2>&1)
37	    local rc=$?
38	
39	    local missing=""
40	    if [[ $rc -ne 0 ]]; then
41	        missing="exit=$rc"
42	    fi
43	    for e in "${expected[@]}"; do
44	        if [[ "$output" != *"$e"* ]]; then
45	            missing+="${missing:+; }missing '$e'"
46	        fi
47	    done
48	
49	    if [[ -z "$missing" ]]; then
50	        printf "ok\n"
51	        pass=$((pass + 1))
52	    else
53	        printf "FAIL — %s\n" "$missing"
54	        fail=$((fail + 1))
55	        failures+=("$map: $missing")
56	    fi
57	}
58	# }}}
59	
60	# {{{ pipeline_output_check() — additional: the pipeline writes to disk
61	pipeline_output_check() {
62	    local file="/tmp/soramech-pipeline-out.txt"
63	    rm -f "$file"
64	    "$DIR/soramech-pool" "$DIR/tests/maps/pipeline" > /dev/null 2>&1
65	    printf "  %-44s " "pipeline file_write output"
66	    if [[ -f "$file" ]]; then
67	        local content
68	        content=$(cat "$file")
69	        if [[ "$content" == "11!" ]]; then
70	            printf "ok\n"
71	            pass=$((pass + 1))
72	        else
73	            printf "FAIL — expected '11!', got '%s'\n" "$content"
74	            fail=$((fail + 1))
75	            failures+=("pipeline file: $content")
76	        fi
77	    else
78	        printf "FAIL — output file missing\n"
79	        fail=$((fail + 1))
80	        failures+=("pipeline: $file missing")
81	    fi
82	}
83	# }}}
84	
85	# {{{ compile_pipeline_check() — soramech-compile produces a portable artifact
86	# Verifies issue 309's compile pipeline: bundles all spec.so + sources
87	# into compiled/, then runs the artifact from /tmp (i.e. with no
88	# dependency on the project's cwd) and verifies it produces the same
89	# output as the source map.
90	compile_pipeline_check() {
91	    local map="$DIR/tests/maps/pipeline"
92	    local compiled="$map/compiled"
93	    local file="/tmp/soramech-pipeline-out.txt"
94	
95	    printf "  %-44s " "compile pipeline (portable run)"
96	
97	    rm -rf "$compiled"
98	    if ! "$DIR/scripts/soramech-compile.sh" "$map" --dir "$DIR" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
99	        printf "FAIL — soramech-compile failed\n"
100	        fail=$((fail + 1))
101	        failures+=("compile: script returned non-zero")
102	        return
103	    fi
104	    if [[ ! -x "$compiled/pool-runner" ]]; then
105	        printf "FAIL — compiled/pool-runner missing or not executable\n"
106	        fail=$((fail + 1))
107	        failures+=("compile: pool-runner missing")
108	        return
109	    fi
110	
111	    rm -f "$file"
112	    local output
113	    output=$(cd /tmp && "$compiled/pool-runner" "$compiled" 2>&1)
114	
115	    local content=""
116	    [[ -f "$file" ]] && content=$(cat "$file")
117	
118	    if [[ "$output" == *"addone → 11"* ]] && [[ "$content" == "11!" ]]; then
119	        printf "ok\n"
120	        pass=$((pass + 1))
121	    else
122	        printf "FAIL — output='%s' file='%s'\n" "$output" "$content"
123	        fail=$((fail + 1))
124	        failures+=("compile: bad output")
125	    fi
126	}
127	# }}}
128	
129	# {{{ run all fixtures
130	echo "integration tests (fixture maps):"
131	
132	check_map "calc" \
133	    "add → 42"
134	
135	check_map "hello" \
136	    "who → World" \
137	    "greet → Hello, World!"
138	
139	check_map "comparator" \
140	    "classify → 8" \
141	    "high → HIGH:8" \
142	    "low → (no output)" \
143	    "mid → (no output)"
144	
145	check_map "iter-route" \
146	    "iter → hi" \
147	    "a → A:hi" \
148	    "b → (no output)" \
149	    "c → (no output)"
150	
151	check_map "pipeline" \
152	    "input → 5" \
153	    "double → 10" \
154	    "addone → 11" \
155	    "shout → 11!"
156	
157	pipeline_output_check
158	compile_pipeline_check
159	# }}}
160	
161	echo
162	echo "  $pass passed, $fail failed"
163	
164	if [[ $fail -gt 0 ]]; then
165	    echo
166	    echo "  failures:"
167	    for f in "${failures[@]}"; do
168	        echo "    - $f"
169	    done
170	fi
171	
172	exit $fail
173	
```

_tool_result:_
```
1	# 231 — Move per-language signature parsers out of the file browser into `langs/<name>/parser.js`
2	
3	## Status
4	open
5	
6	## Current behavior
7	
8	`assets/js/007-filebrowser.js` carries language-specific code:
9	- `parse_lua(content)` — extracts `function M.<name>(<params>)`
10	  declarations and scans for `return` statements to enumerate
11	  outputs.
12	- `parse_bash(content)` — extracts `<name>() {` declarations and
13	  uses `parse_bash_inputs` to count `$N` references.
14	- `parse_functions(content, filename)` — dispatches on file
15	  extension to one of the above.
16	
17	C has no parser yet. Languages a user might add (Python, Rust, …)
18	have no path to a parser.
19	
20	The lexer infrastructure (issue 223, complete) already establishes
21	the right pattern: per-language files under `langs/<name>/`,
22	loaded on demand by the editor through dynamic `import()`. The
23	signature parsers belong there, not in the editor.
24	
25	## Intended behavior
26	
27	Each language directory carries a `parser.js` ES module exporting
28	a single function:
29	
30	```js
31	// langs/<name>/parser.js
32	export function parse_functions(content) {
33	  return [
34	    {
35	      name: 'write_result',
36	      inputs: ['text'],
37	      outputs: [],            // empty array → sink box (issue 226)
38	      variadic_base: null,    // or 'args' for f(a, ...)
39	    },
40	    // ...
41	  ];
42	}
43	```
44	
45	The editor's file browser drops its `parse_lua` / `parse_bash` /
46	`parse_functions` and asks the per-language parser instead. Same
47	lazy-load pattern as the lexers: a registry keyed by file
48	extension, dynamic `import()` on first need, cached per session.
49	Files with an unknown extension fall back to "no parser available"
50	— the file browser displays the file name but offers no
51	function-list step.
52	
53	### Extended return shape
54	
55	The parser returns a richer record than the current shape:
56	- `name` — function name (existing).
57	- `inputs` — array of parameter names in declaration order
58	  (existing).
59	- `outputs` — array of return-value names; empty for sinks
60	  (existing).
61	- `variadic_base` — string or null. Set when the function
62	  declaration has a variadic tail (Lua `...`, Bash `"$@"`, C
63	  `va_list`). Editor uses it to auto-flip the var toggle for that
64	  port. Existing parsers don't compute this; new field, optional
65	  for parsers that don't bother.
66	
67	### Why this matters
68	
69	The file browser is the editor's single entry point for "import
70	function from source." Any language-specific logic in it is the
71	wrong level of abstraction. Moving it puts the editor on equal
72	footing with the runtime: both treat languages as pluggable, both
73	discover capabilities through `langs/<name>/`, neither has a
74	hardcoded list.
75	
76	It also makes adding a new language a self-contained job: drop
77	`langs/<name>/parser.js`, `langs/<name>/lexer.js`, and (phase 3)
78	`langs/<name>/spec.so` into `langs/`, and the editor and the
79	runner both pick it up.
80	
81	## Suggested implementation sequence
82	
83	1. Create `langs/lua/parser.js` with the existing `parse_lua` /
84	   `parse_lua_returns` logic, exporting `parse_functions`.
85	2. Create `langs/bash/parser.js` with the existing `parse_bash` /
86	   `parse_bash_inputs` logic.
87	3. Optionally create `langs/c/parser.js` — a minimal one that
88	   extracts `int name(int a, char *b)` style declarations from
89	   header files. Defer if not needed yet.
90	4. Add a parser registry to `assets/js/007-filebrowser.js` mirroring
91	   the lexer registry in `008-source-view.js`:
92	   ```js
93	   const EXT_TO_LANG = { lua: 'lua', sh: 'bash', bash: 'bash' };
94	   const parser_cache = {};
95	   async function load_parser(lang) { /* dynamic import */ }
96	   ```
97	5. Replace `parse_functions(content, filename)` body with a lookup
98	   into the registry. The function becomes async; existing callers
99	   already `await` async file fetches, so this slots in.
100	6. Delete `parse_lua`, `parse_bash`, `parse_lua_returns`,
101	   `parse_bash_inputs`, `lua_fn_end` from `007-filebrowser.js`.
102	7. (Optional, separate change) Wire `variadic_base` into the
103	   on-select handler so the editor auto-flips the var toggle.
104	
105	## Open questions
106	
107	- **Test coverage**: parsers are easy to break with a regex tweak.
108	  A small set of fixture sources (`maps/_test/parser-fixtures/*.lua`,
109	  `*.sh`) and a JS test harness would catch regressions. Out of
110	  scope for this issue, worth a follow-up.
111	- **Parsing failure shape**: a parser that throws should be
112	  reported as "parser error" not "no parser." Add a try/catch in
113	  the registry layer with a clear message.
114	
115	## Relevant files
116	
117	- `assets/js/007-filebrowser.js` — current parsers move out
118	- `langs/lua/parser.js` — new
119	- `langs/bash/parser.js` — new
120	- `langs/lua/lexer.js`, `langs/bash/lexer.js` — peer modules; same
121	  loading pattern
122	- `assets/js/008-source-view.js` — reference implementation for
123	  the lazy lexer registry
124	- `issues/completed/223-syntax-highlighting-via-user-lexers.md` —
125	  established the `langs/<name>/<file>` server-serve route and the
126	  dynamic-import loading model
127	- `docs/005-language-specs.md` — add a "parser interface" section
128	  alongside the existing "lexer interface"
129	
```

1	-- Text manipulation — shared library and ready-to-use box source.
2	-- require("text") from any Lua function, or copy to a map's src/ to use
3	-- it directly as a call box via the file browser.
4	-- No external dependencies.
5	
6	local M = {}
7	
8	-- {{{ M.concat
9	-- Join N text values with a separator. The signature is variadic so the
10	-- box can grow its `text` input without modifying this source — see
11	-- issue 217 part B for the editor-side variadic-input mechanics.
12	function M.concat(sep, ...)
13	    return table.concat({ ... }, sep or "")
14	end
15	-- }}}
16	
17	-- {{{ M.split
18	-- Split a text into parts on a literal (non-pattern) separator. Returns
19	-- a Lua array of the parts; the driver shim encodes it as JSON for
20	-- transport on the single output wire. The downstream box decodes the
21	-- array — there is no fan-out into multiple wires (that would require
22	-- knowing the part count at design time, which we don't).
23	--
24	-- Plain-string match: the separator is escaped before being passed to
25	-- string.find so users don't have to think about Lua patterns.
26	function M.split(text, sep)
27	    if text == nil then return {} end
28	    if sep == nil or sep == "" then
29	        -- splitting on empty separator: return a single-element array
30	        -- with the whole input. Avoids an infinite loop and matches
31	        -- common library behavior for split-on-empty.
32	        return { text }
33	    end
34	
35	    local parts = {}
36	    local plain = true   -- treat sep as a literal string, not a pattern
37	    local pos   = 1
38	
39	    while true do
40	        local s, e = string.find(text, sep, pos, plain)
41	        if s == nil then
42	            parts[#parts + 1] = string.sub(text, pos)
43	            break
44	        end
45	        parts[#parts + 1] = string.sub(text, pos, s - 1)
46	        pos = e + 1
47	    end
48	
49	    return parts
50	end
51	-- }}}
52	
53	-- Issue 237: the rest of this file is the bundled string-manipulation
54	-- toolbox. All matching is PLAIN (literal) — never Lua-pattern — so
55	-- find/prefix/suffix can carry punctuation without escaping. A user
56	-- who wants real pattern matching can call string.gsub directly via
57	-- a regular call box.
58	
59	-- {{{ M.upper
60	function M.upper(text)
61	    return string.upper(text or "")
62	end
63	-- }}}
64	
65	-- {{{ M.lower
66	function M.lower(text)
67	    return string.lower(text or "")
68	end
69	-- }}}
70	
71	-- {{{ M.trim
72	-- Strip leading and trailing whitespace. `%s` is Lua's whitespace
73	-- class — spaces, tabs, newlines, etc.
74	function M.trim(text)
75	    if text == nil then return "" end
76	    local s = text:gsub("^%s+", ""):gsub("%s+$", "")
77	    return s
78	end
79	-- }}}
80	
81	-- {{{ replace_n
82	-- Internal helper used by M.replace and M.replace_first. Walks the
83	-- text with string.find(..., plain=true) and emits an interleaved
84	-- list of slice + replacement chunks. Avoids string.gsub so we don't
85	-- have to worry about pattern-metacharacter escaping in either the
86	-- search needle or the replacement (which would otherwise need
87	-- separate escape passes — `%(` in needle, `%%` in repl).
88	-- `limit` is the maximum number of replacements (nil = unlimited).
89	local function replace_n(text, find, repl, limit)
90	    if text == nil or find == nil or find == "" then return text or "" end
91	    repl = repl or ""
92	    local out  = {}
93	    local pos  = 1
94	    local hits = 0
95	    while true do
96	        if limit and hits >= limit then
97	            out[#out + 1] = text:sub(pos)
98	            break
99	        end
100	        local s, e = text:find(find, pos, true)
101	        if s == nil then
102	            out[#out + 1] = text:sub(pos)
103	            break
104	        end
105	        out[#out + 1] = text:sub(pos, s - 1)
106	        out[#out + 1] = repl
107	        pos  = e + 1
108	        hits = hits + 1
109	    end
110	    return table.concat(out)
111	end
112	-- }}}
113	
114	-- {{{ M.replace
115	-- Replace every occurrence of `find` with `repl` in `text`.
116	function M.replace(text, find, repl)
117	    return replace_n(text, find, repl, nil)
118	end
119	-- }}}
120	
121	-- {{{ M.replace_first
122	-- Same as M.replace but stops after one replacement. A separate
123	-- function instead of a flag on replace because the editor has no
124	-- boolean-port primitive yet — toggling between two function names
125	-- is clearer than typing "true"/"false" into a third input.
126	function M.replace_first(text, find, repl)
127	    return replace_n(text, find, repl, 1)
128	end
129	-- }}}
130	
131	-- {{{ M.contains
132	-- True if `needle` appears anywhere in `text`. Empty needle returns
133	-- false on the principle that "contains nothing" is a meaningless
134	-- question — a user wiring contains() into a guard almost never
135	-- wants an empty needle to short-circuit truth.
136	function M.contains(text, needle)
137	    if text == nil or needle == nil or needle == "" then return false end
138	    return text:find(needle, 1, true) ~= nil
139	end
140	-- }}}
141	
142	-- {{{ M.starts_with
143	function M.starts_with(text, prefix)
144	    if text == nil or prefix == nil then return false end
145	    if prefix == "" then return true end   -- every string starts with empty
146	    return text:sub(1, #prefix) == prefix
147	end
148	-- }}}
149	
150	-- {{{ M.ends_with
151	function M.ends_with(text, suffix)
152	    if text == nil or suffix == nil then return false end
153	    if suffix == "" then return true end   -- every string ends with empty
154	    return text:sub(-#suffix) == suffix
155	end
156	-- }}}
157	
158	-- {{{ M.length
159	function M.length(text)
160	    if text == nil then return 0 end
161	    return #text
162	end
163	-- }}}
164	
165	-- {{{ M.substring
166	-- Substring starting at `start` (1-indexed) of length `len`. If `len`
167	-- is omitted, takes everything from `start` to the end. Out-of-range
168	-- positions follow string.sub semantics (clamped, not errored).
169	function M.substring(text, start, len)
170	    if text == nil then return "" end
171	    start = tonumber(start) or 1
172	    len   = tonumber(len)
173	    if len == nil then return text:sub(start) end
174	    return text:sub(start, start + len - 1)
175	end
176	-- }}}
177	
178	return M
179

tests/232-lua-parser-test.mjs\n\nimport { test } from 'node:test';\nimport { strict as assert } from 'node:assert';\n\n// DIR is hardcoded so the script runs regardless of cwd; first arg\n// overrides assert.equal(fns[0].variadic_tail, true);\n});\n// }}}\n\n// {{{ test('non-variadic function reports variadic_tail=false')\ntest('non-variadic function reports variadic_tail=false', () => {\n  const src = `\nfunction M.split(text, sep)\n    return parts\nend\n`;\n  const fns = parse_functions(src);\n  assert.equal(fns.length, 1);\n  assert.equal(fns[0].name, 'split');\n empty inputs')\ntest('parameterless function has empty inputs', () => {\n  const src = `\nfunction M.no_args()\n    return 1\nend\n`;\n  const fns = parse_functions(src);\n  assert.equal(fns.length, tests/233-bash-parser-test.mjs\n\nimport { test } from 'node:test';\nimport { strict as assert } from 'node:assert';\n\nconst DIR = process.argv[2] || '/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech';\n\nconst { parse_functions } = await import(`${DIR}/langs/bash/parser.js`);\n\n// {{{ test('\"$@\" reference flips variadic_tail on')\ntest('\"$@\" reference flips variadic_tail on', () => {\n  const src = `\neach_arg() {\n    local prefix=\"$1\"\n    shift\n    for x in \"$@\"; do\n        printf '%s%s\\\\n' \"$prefix\" \"$x\"\n    done\n}\n`;\n  const fns = parse_functions(src);\n  assert.equal(fns.length, 1);\n  assert.equal(fns[0].name, 'each_arg');\n  assert.equal(fns[0].variadic_tail, true);\n  assert.deepEqual(fns[0].outputs, ['output']);\n});\n// }}}\n\n// {{{ test('locals named from positional params become input names')\ntest('locals named from positional params become input names', () => {\n  const src = `\nwrite_msg() {\n    local path=\"$1\"\n    local body=\"$2\"\n    printf '%s\\\\n' \"$body\" > \"$path\"\n}\n`;\n  const fns = parse_functions(src);\n  assert.equal(fns.length, 1);\n  assert.deepEqual(fns[0].inputs, ['path', 'body']);\n  assert.equal(fns[0].variadic_tail, false);\n});\n// }}}\n\n// {{{ test('positional refs without locals fall back to argN')\ntest('positional refs without locals fall back to argN', () => {\n  const src = `\nshout() {\n    printf '%s!' \"$1\"\n}\n`;\n  const fns = parse_functions(src);\n  assert.deepEqual(fns[0].inputs, ['arg1']);\n  assert.equal(fns[0].variadic_tail, false);\n});\n// }}}\n\n// {{{ test('gaps in local bindings are filled with argN placeholders')\ntest('gaps in local bindings are filled with argN placeholders', () => {\n  // Only $1 and $3 named — $2 should appear as arg2 so port order\n  // stays positional.\n  const src = `\nsparse() {\n    local first=\"$1\"\n    local third=\"$3\"\n}\n`;\n  const fns = parse_functions(src);\n  assert.deepEqual(fns[0].inputs, ['first', 'arg2', 'third']);\n});\n// }}}\n\n// {{{ test('no positional refs at all means empty inputs')\ntest('no positional refs at all means empty inputs', () => {\n  const src = `\ngreet() {\n    echo \"hello\"\n}\n`;\n  const fns = parse_functions(src);\n  assert.deepEqual(fns[0].inputs, []);\n  assert.equal(fns[0].variadic_tail, false);\n});\n// }}}\n\n// {{{ test('control-flow keywords that look - `tests/232-lua-parser-test.mjs` — 10 cases covering `variadic_tail` detection, `...` stripping from inputs, return-statement output naming, nested-block end-matching, `repeat/until`, M.-only filtering, declaration order, and the empty case.
- `tests/233-bash-parser-test.mjs` — 9 cases covering `"$@"` → variadic, named locals → input names, gap-filling with `argN`, fallback naming with no locals, control-flow skip set, `${N}` braced refs, declaration order, and empty.
- `scripts/run-tests.sh` — new `parser_tests()` section runs `node --test` on each file and folds pass/fail into the existing counters.

- `issues/phase-2-progress.md` — 232 added as complete.

Both parsers tested via `bash scripts/run-tests.sh`. Verified failure detection by temporarily breaking the Lua parser's variadic-tail check — the test surfaced it as expected. The runner uses `SORAMECH_DIR=...` to pass the project root (positional argv would collide with `node --test`'s file list).

1	# 316 — Simple test runner script
2	
3	## Status
4	open
5	
6	## Current behavior
7	
8	`make test` builds and runs every unit-test binary plus the
9	integration runner. The output is helpful when you want to verify
10	that everything still passes at the end of a working session, but
11	it's noisy when you're iterating on one specific change:
12	
13	- The build phase prints `→ src/foo.c`, `→ src/bar.c`, ... for
14	  every file that compiles or relinks. On a clean tree that's
15	  dozens of lines before the first test runs.
16	- Tests are run from inside a Make recipe (a `for` loop). If one
17	  binary fails, the recipe aborts and you don't see the results
18	  for binaries that would have run after it.
19	- The integration runner (`scripts/run-tests.sh`) is interleaved
20	  with unit tests, mixing two scales of test output in one stream.
21	
22	The current behavior is fine for "ship it" verification. It's
23	suboptimal as a development loop.
24	
25	## Intended behavior
26	
27	A separate script (`scripts/run-unit-tests.sh`) that runs the
28	already-built unit-test binaries one by one and reports their
29	results in a uniform format. No build step. No make. Just:
30	locate the binaries, run them in turn, capture stdout/stderr,
31	print a one-line per-binary summary, and at the end print a
32	totals line and a list of any failures.
33	
34	Goals:
35	
36	1. **Cheap to invoke.** No make recursion, no spec rebuilds,
37	   no integration tests. If the build is current, the script
38	   runs in well under a second.
39	2. **Continues past failures.** Every test binary runs, even if
40	   an earlier one fails. The summary at the bottom names what
41	   went wrong.
42	3. **Pretty output by default; mineable output on request.** A
43	   `--quiet` mode that prints just the totals line is enough for
44	   CI pre-merge gates. A `--verbose` mode dumps each binary's
45	   full output. The default is per-binary one-line status.
46	4. **Filterable.** A positional argument like
47	   `scripts/run-unit-tests.sh 009` runs only the binaries whose
48	   names start with `009`, so iterating on one slot-store change
49	   doesn't require re-running every test.
50	
51	Out of scope: building the binaries, running integration tests
52	(that's `scripts/run-tests.sh`), running specs (those are
53	implicitly tested via the binaries that depend on them).
54	
55	## Concept
56	
57	The build system already produces `build/tests/<name>-test`
58	binaries; one per test source file under `tests/`. The script
59	discovers them via a glob, sorts them by filename (which sorts
60	by leading numeric index, which is the project's natural
61	read-order), and runs each one.
62	
63	Each binary already prints its own header (`009-slot-store-test:`)
64	and per-test status lines. The script captures the output, parses
65	the last line (`N passed, M failed`) for the per-binary verdict,
66	and prints a per-binary summary like:
67	
68	```
69	  009-slot-store-test                          16 / 16  ok
70	  010-graph-loader-test                        13 / 13  ok
71	  015-large-value-heap-test                     8 /  8  ok
72	  ...
73	```
74	
75	A failing binary prints its captured output below the summary
76	line so the failure is visible without re-running.
77	
78	The very last line is:
79	
80	```
81	  113 / 113 tests passed (14 binaries)
82	```
83	
84	…or, on failure:
85	
86	```
87	  111 / 113 tests passed (14 binaries; 1 binary with failures: 009-slot-store-test)
88	```
89	
90	Exit code: zero if every binary passes; non-zero with the count
91	of failing binaries otherwise.
92	
93	## Convention compliance
94	
95	Per the project's script rules (CLAUDE.md):
96	
97	- Hard-coded `${DIR}` at the top; first argument optionally
98	  overrides it.
99	- All paths under `${DIR}`.
100	- Each shell command on its own line (no chained `||` /
101	  `&&` pipelines for control flow; assign command output to
102	  variables and branch on the variable).
103	- Lives at `scripts/run-unit-tests.sh`, runnable from any cwd.
104	
105	## Suggested implementation steps
106	
107	1. Write `scripts/run-unit-tests.sh` with the layout described
108	   above.
109	2. Verify it picks up every existing `build/tests/*-test`
110	   binary and produces the expected totals.
111	3. Verify the filter argument works: `scripts/run-unit-tests.sh
112	   015` runs just the lvh test binary.
113	4. Verify `--quiet` and `--verbose` modes both produce useful
114	   output.
115	5. Provoke a failure (modify one test temporarily) and confirm
116	   the failure is reported, exit code is non-zero, and the
117	   captured output is visible.
118	6. Document the script in `Makefile`'s `help` target, or add a
119	   `make quicktest` alias that calls it.
120	
121	## Relevant files
122	
123	- `scripts/run-unit-tests.sh` — new
124	- `Makefile` — optional `quicktest` target
125	- `scripts/run-tests.sh` — sibling script for integration tests;
126	  this issue covers the unit-test counterpart
127	
128	## Open questions
129	
130	- Should the script also verify the binaries are up to date with
131	  their sources? Cheap: `stat` the binary and its `.c` and
132	  complain if the source is newer. The user might prefer "just
133	  run what's built" so they can deliberately test old binaries.
134	  Default to no warning; offer a `--check-stale` flag.
135	- Filter syntax: prefix-match on the test name is simple; should
136	  it accept glob patterns too? Start simple. Extend if needed.
137	
```

```
1	# 311 — Integration tests & run output (`last-run.json`)
2	
3	## Status
4	open
5	
6	## Current behavior
7	
8	Phase 2 writes a `last-run.json` after every map run, recording per-
9	box invocations, inputs, outputs, and any errors. The editor reads
10	it to display a run summary. Phase 2 testing is ad-hoc: a few maps
11	in `maps/` (`hello`, `branch-test`, `classify-demo`, `driver-test`)
12	that the developer runs by hand to verify the synchronous executor
13	still works.
14	
15	Phase 3 changes the shape of the problem: many concurrent
16	invocations, possibly thousands per run when iterators are in play,
17	written from multiple worker threads. The phase 2 "build a single
18	table at end of run" approach does not survive that. And the
19	existing maps cover only a small slice of the new surface (no
20	queued inputs, no large iterator counts, no fan-in).
21	
22	This issue covers two things that go together: the format and
23	writer for the run output, and the integration test plan that
24	exercises the phase 3 runtime end-to-end.
25	
26	## Part A — Run output
27	
28	### Decoupling from the editor
29	
30	The editor (phase 2) is being scoped down to a pure map editor — it
31	does not know how to run maps and does not display run results.
32	`last-run.json` is therefore not a UI feed. It exists for:
33	
34	- LLM consumption — agents reading the file to understand what a
35	  run did
36	- CLI-driven inspection — a future viewer utility (out of scope
37	  here) that pretty-prints the file
38	- Test harness consumption — the integration tests below diff the
39	  written file against expected output
40	
41	Decoupling means the writer's design is driven by these consumers,
42	not by editor rendering needs.
43	
44	### File format: JSON Lines
45	
46	The phase 2 single-document format does not work when the runner is
47	streaming events from many threads. Use JSON Lines instead — one
48	JSON object per line, each line is one event, the file grows
49	monotonically as the run proceeds.
50	
51	Event types:
52	
53	```jsonl
54	{"event":"run_start", "ts":1234567890.123, "map":"maps/classify-demo", "n_workers":16}
55	{"event":"task_submit", "ts":..., "task_id":42, "box_id":"classify", "worker_idx":-1}
56	{"event":"task_start", "ts":..., "task_id":42, "worker_idx":3}
57	{"event":"task_end", "ts":..., "task_id":42, "worker_idx":3, "duration_us":127, "output_size":12}
58	{"event":"slot_alloc", "ts":..., "slot_id":5, "size":256, "n_cells":1}
59	{"event":"slot_free", "ts":..., "slot_id":5}
60	{"event":"run_end", "ts":..., "duration_us":1850, "n_tasks":7}
61	```
62	
63	JSONL is append-only, which makes thread-safe writing tractable: a
64	single mutex around the write call serializes the lines, and each
65	line is self-contained so a partial line on crash is recoverable
66	(reader skips the broken trailing line).
67	
68	The reader (LLM, viewer, test harness) parses one line at a time;
69	total file size doesn't have to fit in memory.
70	
71	### What gets logged
72	
73	Per-task: submission, start, end, duration, output size. Inputs
74	and outputs are NOT logged by default — for iterator boxes
75	producing thousands of invocations, logging every value bloats
76	the file unbounded. There is a verbosity switch:
77	
78	- `SORAMECH_LOG_VALUES=1` — log inputs and outputs (truncated past
79	  some byte limit, e.g. 4 KB) per task. For debugging.
80	- Default: log task lifecycle but not values.
81	
82	Slot allocator events (alloc, free, coalesce, grow) at a separate
83	verbosity level. Off by default; on under `SORAMECH_LOG_SLOTS=1`.
84	For diagnosing memory issues.
85	
86	### Writer mechanism
87	
88	A single dedicated writer thread inside the runner consumes events
89	from a multi-producer single-consumer ring buffer. Worker threads
90	push event records (small fixed-size structs) into the ring; the
91	writer pulls them, serializes to JSON, and writes lines to the
92	file. This avoids per-event mutex contention — the ring buffer is
93	the only shared state, and a well-written MPSC ring is lock-free
94	on the producer side.
95	
96	Alternative considered: each worker writes its own log file, merge
97	at end. Simpler concurrency, but readers would have to merge by
98	timestamp — and any cross-event reasoning (was task A still
99	running when task B ended?) requires the merge anyway.
100	
101	The writer thread is started after the pool is created and joined
102	before `pool_destroy`. It also handles `run_end` as the very last
103	event, including the final summary.
104	
105	### File location
106	
107	`<map-dir>/last-run.jsonl` (note: `.jsonl` not `.json` — the
108	extension reflects the format change). Overwritten on every run.
109	
110	A rotated archive (`last-run.jsonl.1`, `.2`, …) is out of scope.
111	Users who want history copy the file before re-running.
112	
113	## Part B — Integration tests
114	
115	### Scope
116	
117	End-to-end tests of the pool runner against real map directories.
118	Each test is:
119	
120	1. A map directory under `tests/maps/<name>/`
121	2. An expected `last-run.jsonl` (or a comparison spec) under
122	   `tests/expected/<name>.jsonl`
123	3. A test driver script that runs the map and diffs
124	
125	The test driver is `scripts/run-tests.sh`. It iterates
126	`tests/maps/`, runs each, diffs against expected (with a normalizer
127	that drops timestamps, durations, worker indices — anything non-
128	deterministic), and reports pass/fail.
129	
130	### Test maps
131	
132	Coverage targets:
133	
134	| Test map                  | What it exercises                                    |
135	|---------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|
136	| `hello`                   | Single Lua box, one input, one output                |
137	| `data-only`               | Data box → call box. No inputs, just stored values   |
138	| `fan-out-3`               | One output → three consumer boxes (peek, refcount)   |
139	| `fan-in-iterator`         | Three producers → iterator with queued input         |
140	| `comparator-routing`      | Comparator with all three branches (lt/eq/gt)        |
141	| `iterator-100`            | Iterator with 100 input values, round-robin to 3 paths |
142	| `multilang`               | One Lua box, one C box, one Bash box, all wired      |
143	| `large-output`            | Box producing a 1 MB string (variable-size heap)     |
144	| `cycle-detector-fail`     | Non-iterator cycle — load must abort                 |
145	| `missing-input-fail`      | Connection references a nonexistent box — load aborts|
146	| `box-error-fail`          | Box function returns nonzero — runner aborts         |
147	
148	The `*-fail` tests verify error behavior — the runner is expected
149	to exit nonzero with a precise error message. The driver matches
150	exit code and stderr against expected.
151	
152	### Test driver
153	
154	```bash
155	# scripts/run-tests.sh
156	for test_dir in tests/maps/*/; do
157	    name=$(basename "${test_dir}")
158	    expected="tests/expected/${name}.jsonl"
159	
160	    actual=$( "${SOURCE_DIR}/soramech-pool" "${test_dir}" )
161	    rc=$?
162	
163	    if [ -f "${expected}.exit" ]; then
164	        # *-fail tests: assert exit code and stderr substring
165	        check_failure "${name}" "${rc}" "${test_dir}/last-run.jsonl"
166	    else
167	        # success tests: diff normalized last-run.jsonl
168	        check_success "${name}" "${test_dir}/last-run.jsonl" "${expected}"
169	    fi
170	done
171	```
172	
173	`normalize_jsonl` strips timestamps, durations, worker indices,
174	slot ids, and task ids (all non-deterministic) and sorts events by
175	event type. The result should be deterministic across runs.
176	
177	### Phase 3 demo map
178	
179	A single map under `issues/completed/demos/phase-3/` that
180	demonstrates everything working together:
181	
182	- Multiple entry-point data boxes
183	- Lua, C, and Bash boxes all wired together
184	- An iterator with a queued input
185	- A comparator routing into different downstream paths
186	- A large-output box (variable-size heap)
187	- Non-trivial graph topology (≥ 10 boxes)
188	
189	Run with `./run.sh` from the demo directory. Prints a short summary
190	of what happened (parsing the `last-run.jsonl`) so the demo is
191	visible without a viewer utility.
192	
193	This satisfies the standing project rule (CLAUDE.md) that each
194	phase produces a runnable demo in `issues/completed/demos/`.
195	
196	### Test infrastructure constraints
197	
198	- Tests must not depend on the editor or any HTTP server
199	- Tests must not depend on internet access
200	- Tests must run in under 60 seconds total on a developer machine
201	- Tests must be deterministic when normalized (any
202	  non-determinism is normalized out, not papered over with retries)
203	
204	## Open questions
205	
206	- Whether to log inputs/outputs by default. Useful for development,
207	  noisy for production. Current decision: opt-in via env var,
208	  default off.
209	- Slot-event logging: also opt-in. Useful for diagnosing memory
210	  issues. Off by default.
211	- File format: JSON Lines is the choice. An alternative was a
212	  protobuf or msgpack binary log, which is faster to write but
213	  harder to inspect by hand. JSONL wins on inspectability.
214	- Whether to keep timestamps as Unix epoch floats or ISO 8601
215	  strings. Epoch floats are smaller and easier to diff. Stick with
216	  floats.
217	
218	## Suggested implementation sequence
219	
220	### Run output (Part A)
221	1. Define the event struct and JSONL line format. Start with
222	   `run_start`, `task_start`, `task_end`, `run_end`.
223	2. Implement the MPSC ring buffer for events.
224	3. Implement the writer thread: pulls from ring, serializes,
225	   appends a line to the output file.
226	4. Wire event emission into the pool runner: `run_start` at
227	   startup, `task_start` and `task_end` in the dispatch action,
228	   `run_end` after quiescence.
229	5. Add the verbosity env vars and the optional event types.
230	
231	### Tests (Part B)
232	6. Create `tests/maps/` and `tests/expected/` directory structure.
233	7. Write the `hello` test as the first end-to-end smoke check.
234	8. Write the test driver `scripts/run-tests.sh` and the JSONL
235	   normalizer.
236	9. Add tests one at a time as each runtime feature lands. The
237	   `*-fail` tests can land before their corresponding successful
238	   tests if the failure path is implemented first.
239	10. Build the phase 3 demo map under `issues/completed/demos/phase-3/`
240	    once the rest of phase 3 is implemented.
241	
242	## Relevant files
243	
244	- `scripts/run-tests.sh` — test driver (to be written)
245	- `tests/maps/`, `tests/expected/` — test fixtures (to be created)
246	- `issues/completed/demos/phase-3/` — phase 3 demo (to be built)
247	- `src/008-pool-runner.c` — emits run_start / run_end
248	- `src/dispatch.c` — emits task_start / task_end
249	- `src/slot-store.c` — emits slot_alloc / slot_free (verbose mode)
250	- `issues/301-pool-lifecycle-and-worker-init.md` — pool lifecycle
251	- `issues/302-wire-value-slot-store.md` — slot events
252	- `issues/304-task-dispatch-layer.md` — task lifecycle events
253	- `issues/305-c-graph-loader.md` — load-time errors
254	- `issues/309-build-system.md` — `make test` invokes this
255	- `docs/004-ipc-and-threading.md` — blocking semantics relevant to
256	  test design
257	
258	## Implementation log
259	
260	### JSON writer half — 2026-05-12
261	
262	`libs/json/json.c` had stub writer bodies left over from 314's
263	parser milestone; this pass filled them in. The writer maintains
264	a small depth stack (capped at 16) where each entry packs two
265	booleans — "next element is first" and "this level is an
266	object." That's enough to handle comma placement and pick the
267	right close bracket. Overflow of the caller-supplied buffer is
268	sticky on `w->err`; `json_writer_finish` reports it.
269	
270	Eight new writer tests in `tests/314-json-test.c` cover the
271	primitives, escape encoding, empty and non-empty containers,
272	nesting depth, overflow detection, and a round-trip where the
273	writer emits an object that the parser then decodes and the test
274	verifies via the accessor API.
275	
276	Still ahead for 311: the JSONL event writer (a thin wrapper that
277	serializes `task_start` / `task_end` / `run_start` / `run_end`
278	records using this writer), the MPSC ring buffer the worker
279	threads push events into, and the dedicated writer thread that
280	drains them.
281	
282	### Event emitters — 2026-05-12
283	
284	`src/013-jsonl-events.{c,h}` ships the per-event emit functions
285	on top of issue 314's bounded writer:
286	
287	- `jsonl_emit_run_start(w, ts, map, n_workers)`
288	- `jsonl_emit_task_submit(w, ts, task_id, box_id, worker_idx)`
289	- `jsonl_emit_task_start(w, ts, task_id, worker_idx)`
290	- `jsonl_emit_task_end(w, ts, task_id, worker_idx, duration_us, output_size)`
291	- `jsonl_emit_run_end(w, ts, duration_us, n_tasks)`
292	
293	Each emit serializes into a fixed 512-byte buffer, appends a
294	newline, and writes to a `FILE *` under a per-writer mutex. The
295	MPSC ring + dedicated writer thread aren't there yet — the mutex
296	is a placeholder until dispatch is producing events fast enough
297	to justify the contention.
298	
299	`tests/013-jsonl-events-test.c` writes a full sequence, slurps
300	the file back, and parses each line through 314's JSON parser to
301	verify the event shape. A concurrent-emit test runs 8 producer
302	threads each emitting 200 events into the same writer and
303	verifies every line round-trips.
304	
305	Still ahead inside 311: slot-event opt-in
306	(`SORAMECH_LOG_SLOTS=1`), inputs/outputs opt-in
307	(`SORAMECH_LOG_VALUES=1`), the MPSC ring + writer thread, and
308	the integration-test harness map fixtures.
309	
310	### Multi-producer event queue + writer thread — 2026-05-12
311	
312	`src/014-event-queue.{c,h}` ships the producer-side queue and the
313	dedicated writer thread. Producers (worker threads + the main
314	thread) call `event_queue_run_start` / `task_submit` / `task_start`
315	/ `task_end` / `run_end`; each emit appends a small fixed-size
316	event record under a brief mutex and signals a CV. The writer
317	thread sleeps on the CV, snapshots the queue head into a local
318	batch under the lock, then drains the batch outside the lock —
319	JSON encoding + fwrite happen with zero producer contention.
320	
321	Four unit tests in `tests/014-event-queue-test.c` cover single
322	producer / multi-thread burst (8 producers × 250 events each =
323	2000 lines, all round-trip through the JSON parser) / shutdown-
324	drain / pending-count.
325	
326	The dispatch action (issue 304) opt-in emits task_start and
327	task_end events with monotonic-clock durations via the queue;
328	the pool runner emits run_start and run_end. `soramech-pool`
329	writes to `map_dir/tmp/last-run.jsonl` when that directory
330	exists, falling back to `/tmp/soramech-last-run.jsonl`.
331	
332	What's still ahead inside 311:
333	- Lock-free Vyukov MPSC ring with sequence numbers — the
334	  architecture doc's preferred design. The mutex queue is a
335	  placeholder; upgrade once contention shows up.
336	- Opt-in slot events and per-task input/output dumps.
337	
338	### Integration test runner — 2026-05-12
339	
340	`scripts/run-tests.sh` runs every fixture map under `tests/maps/`
341	through `soramech-pool` and asserts the expected box-output
342	substrings show up in stderr. `make test` invokes the script
343	after the unit tests so a full `make test` covers everything
344	from slot operations all the way through end-to-end Lua / C /
345	Bash dispatch via JSONL logging.
346	
347	Six fixture checks at the moment:
348	- `calc`            — single Lua `add → 42`
349	- `hello`           — data → Lua, `greet → "Hello, World!"`
350	- `comparator`      — `high` fires, `low` / `mid` don't
351	- `iter-route`      — single-fire iterator picks `out_0` → `a`
352	- `pipeline`        — five-box multilang chain
353	- `pipeline file`   — verifies the file_write sink wrote
354	  `"11!"` to `/tmp/soramech-pipeline-out.txt`
355	
```

Argv would collide with `node --test`'s\nfile-list parsing, so an env var is the right channel.\n\nVerified failure detection by temporarily inverting the Lua\nparser's variadic-tail check — the lua test failed as expected and\nthe failure surfaced through the shell runner.\n\n## Relevant files\n\n- `scripts/run-tests.sh` — the integration + JS test driver\n- `tests/232-lua-parser-test.mjs`, `tests/233-bash-parser-test.mjs`\n- `issues/311-integration-tests-and-run-output.md` — phase-3\n  integration tests and JSONL format\n- `issues/316-simple-test-runner.md` — planned C unit-test runner\n  script\n- `issues/231-move-signature-parsers-into-langs.md` — moved the\n  parsers under test into `langs/<name>/parser.js`\n- `issues/217-concat-box-and-dynamic-inputs.md` — part C consumes\n  the `variadic_tail` field these tests guard\n",

ts: 2026-05-19T23:38:32.414Z

Issue 217 — "Concat box with per-input variadic ports and auto-grow/shrink." Currently in `issues/` (reopened for Part C).

**Three parts, layered:**

**Part A** *(shipped)* — `libs/text.lua` exposes `M.concat(sep, ...)` and `M.split(text, sep)`. `concat` is variadic so its `text` input can grow without source edits. `split` returns a Lua array; the driver shim JSON-encodes for the single output wire (multi-wire fan-out was deliberately rejected — output count isn't known at design time).

**Part B** *(shipped)* — per-input variadic UI in `assets/js/004-inspector.js`. A box gets a `variadic_inputs` array of base names; slots are stored as `<base>_<index>` in `inputs`. Helpers cover the lifecycle: `make_variadic` (rename `text` → `text_0`), `unmake_variadic` (collapse back), `add_variadic_slot` (auto-grow on the last slot), `remove_variadic_slot` (delete + renumber). Schema accepts `variadic_inputs` (`src/001-schema.lua`). Auto-grow triggers on wire-connect and value-set; shrink is **only** via explicit `×` button — deliberate, so transient editing doesn't lose structure.

**Part C** *(open, was blocked on 231)* — reject the `var` toggle on a non-variadic parameter. Today the toggle is user-asserted; if the underlying function doesn't accept varargs, extra positional args are silently swallowed (Lua/Bash drop trailing args; C errors or ignores). This is the silent-error class issue 230 closed off — same shape, different surface.

The fix: the inspector asks `langs/<name>/parser.js` for the function's signature when rendering a `call` box. If the port name is in the parser's `variadic_tail`-implied list, show the `var` button; otherwise hide it. The schema/validator does the same check at compile time (issue 309) so pre-existing maps get caught.

**Status note worth flagging:** 217 says Part C is "blocked on issue 231," but 231 has landed — `langs/lua/parser.js` and `langs/bash/parser.js` exist and export `variadic_tail` (the exact field Part C wants). The new parser tests (issue 232) guard the `variadic_tail` contract specifically because Part C will lean on it. So Part C is **unblocked** — just unimplemented. The blockers line in 217 should be updated, and the inspector + validator changes from "Implementation steps (post-231)" can begin.

Want me to update 217's status line and start on the Part C implementation? The work is well-scoped: pass `variadic_tail` from the file-browser's on-select handler through to `mk_port_display`, gate the `var` button render, and mirror the check in `src/001-schema.lua` / `src/002-validate-map.lua`.

### [218] type=assistant
ts: 2026-05-19T23:46:27.632Z

You're right and I was rebuilding the same crystal I was supposed to break.

**On 217 being stale.** Multiple axes:
- "Concat box" is wrong framing — there's no special box kind, it's just `M.concat` in `libs/text.lua` invoked by any call box. The issue title and Part A still talk like there's a dedicated box.
- "Split returns a JSON-encoded array on the single output wire" is also stale. That was the phase-2 transport story. Phase 3 (issue 312, same-language fast path) classifies edges and only JSON-encodes when crossing a language boundary. So `M.split` returning a Lua array is correct when the downstream box is Lua, and the runtime handles encoding when it isn't. The issue's framing predates that mechanism.
- Honestly, `split` without a fan-out primitive is awkward on its own. The canonical pattern is `split → iterator (221)` so each part lands on its own dispatch. Outside that pairing it's just "an array on a wire."

**On `variadic_tail` per-function detection — yes, you're right, it doesn't work.** Let me actually re-derive why instead of waving:

- Every Lua function already accepts N positional args. `function f(a)` with 5 args passed: 4 silently dropped. The `...` in the signature is the function declaring it *cares* about extras, not declaring whether the *runtime* can pass them. Same in Bash — every function gets `$@` whether it touches it or not. Same in C through the dispatch wrapper — `argc`/`argv` is the universal call shape (issue 304).
- So "is this function variadic" is the wrong question. Every function in every supported language is variadic at the calling-convention level. What varies is what the function does with extras.
- Putting that distinction in `parser.js` means the editor cracks open source files and runs language-specific regex to answer a question the runtime doesn't even care about. That's exactly the per-language ossification the language-spec model was supposed to replace.
- And it can't extend. Adding Python means writing a Python parser. Adding Rust means writing a Rust parser. Each one has to encode that language's idea of "variadic-ness" in a regex.

**Update on language-spec-handles-variadic: there is none.** It's in 217's "Future development ideas" section as a sketch, and we discussed it, but no spec field exists. No `langs/<name>/spec.c` declares a variadic shape. The parser.js work I just shipped under 231/232 went the **opposite** direction — it put per-function detection in the editor. I locked the wrong contract in with tests.

**Where the right direction actually goes:**

The language spec declares its calling convention shape — that's already implicit in how `spec.c` unpacks `argc`/`argv` for the dispatch action. We make it explicit: every spec declares that it accepts N positional args and what shape the language exposes them as (Lua `...`, Bash `"$@"`, C `argv[]`). That declaration is one constant per spec, not per-function. The editor reads it the same way it loads the lexer — once per language.

The `var` toggle on a port then doesn't ask "does this function accept varargs," it asks "does this language support variadic at the calling convention." For every shipped language so far, the answer is yes. The toggle is enabled universally.

If a user wires 5 inputs to a function that internally only reads the first, that's the user's mistake — same class as wiring a string into a port the function treats as a number. The runtime doesn't police it; the editor surfaces it via documentation (the parsed signature shows `f(a)`, so the user sees the arity), not via gating.

That collapses Part C from "block the toggle" to "show the signature clearly enough that the user sees what they're doing."

**On the new tests** — `tests/232-lua-parser-test.mjs` and `tests/233-bash-parser-test.mjs` assert `variadic_tail` round-trips. If we drop the field from `parser.js` (which we should, since it was answering the wrong question), those assertions go with it. The remaining tests — input names, output names, declaration order, nested-block handling — are still worth keeping. Roughly half each file gets trimmed.

**Concrete next moves, if you want to walk this back:**

1. Add a `variadic_shape` constant to each `langs/<name>/spec.c` (positional, for all three shipped languages today).
2. Drop `variadic_tail` from both `parser.js` files and trim those assertions from the new tests.
3. Rewrite 217 from scratch — the concat-box framing, split's JSON story, and Part C are all wrong. The replacement is short: "variadic is a property of the language calling convention; the spec declares it; the editor reads the spec; the `var` toggle is gated by the spec, not by source parsing."
4. Update the test coverage map (232) so the parser tests' scope reflects the trimmed reality.

Before I touch any of that — does that match what you were gesturing at, or am I still missing something? I want to think with you on this rather than barrel into another wrong direction.

1	/* langs/lang-spec.h — public interface for SoraMech language specs.
2	 *
3	 * What it is, in a sentence: the C contract that every language
4	 * spec (Lua, C, Bash, anything user-added) implements. The pool
5	 * runner discovers specs by dlopen()ing langs/<name>/spec.so at
6	 * startup and reading the exported soramech_lang_spec symbol.
7	 *
8	 * Designed in issue 303. The init barrier (issue 301) calls init()
9	 * for every spec on every worker before unblocking task dispatch;
10	 * the dispatch action (issue 304) calls invoke() per task; the
11	 * compile-button pipeline (issue 309) calls compile() per source
12	 * file for languages whose compile pointer is non-NULL.
13	 *
14	 * Errors: a nonzero return from any callback is fatal. The dispatch
15	 * layer prints the error context and aborts. There is no recovery,
16	 * no retry, no per-task failed state. This is intentional.
17	 *
18	 * Issue 312 extends this contract with optional fast-path
19	 * callbacks: `invoke_native` and `invoke_json` are specialised
20	 * variants the dispatch layer prefers when wire classification
21	 * says they apply; `native_to_json` and `json_to_native` are
22	 * bridges used at language boundaries. Specs that don't set them
23	 * fall back to `invoke` for every call.
24	 */
25	
26	#ifndef SORAMECH_LANG_SPEC_H
27	#define SORAMECH_LANG_SPEC_H
28	
29	#include <stddef.h>
30	
31	#ifdef __cplusplus
32	extern "C" {
33	#endif
34	
35	/* {{{ Shared signatures */
36	/* Invoke signature — shared by `invoke`, `invoke_native`, and
37	 * `invoke_json`. The dispatch layer picks which one to call based
38	 * on the wire-format classification in issue 312. */
39	typedef int (*lang_invoke_fn)(void *handle,
40	                              const char *file_path,
41	                              const char *fn_name,
42	                              const void **input_data,
43	                              const int   *input_sizes,
44	                              int          n_inputs,
45	                              void        *out_buf,
46	                              int          out_buf_capacity,
47	                              int         *out_size);
48	
49	/* Wire-format bridge: convert one direction of native ↔ JSON.
50	 * Used when a value crosses a language boundary. Spec sets one
51	 * or both; if NULL, the dispatch layer treats native and JSON as
52	 * identical byte streams (the current uniform-byte default). */
53	typedef int (*lang_bridge_fn)(const void *src, int src_size,
54	                              void *dst, int dst_capacity, int *dst_size);
55	/* }}} */
56	
57	/* {{{ lang_spec_t */
58	typedef struct lang_spec {
59	    const char *name;       /* "lua", "bash", "c", "python", ... */
60	    const char *file_ext;   /* ".lua", ".sh", ".c", ".py"        */
61	
62	    /* init: once per worker thread at pool startup. Returns an
63	     * opaque handle stored in the worker context; the dispatch
64	     * layer passes this handle back to invoke() for every call. */
65	    void *(*init)(int worker_idx);
66	
67	    /* teardown: once per worker at pool shutdown. Frees the handle
68	     * and any per-worker resources (e.g. lua_State, dlopen caches,
69	     * persistent socket connections). */
70	    void  (*teardown)(void *handle);
71	
72	    /* compile: optional. Called once per source file at map-load
73	     * (or at editor compile-button time). For languages that don't
74	     * compile (Lua, Bash), leave this NULL. For C, it runs
75	     * gcc -shared -fPIC -o out_path src_path. Returns 0 on
76	     * success, nonzero on failure. */
77	    int   (*compile)(const char *src_path, const char *out_path);
78	
79	    /* invoke: per box call. Inputs are arrays of (bytes, size) pairs,
80	     * already read from slots by the dispatch layer. Returns the
81	     * function's output by writing to out_buf and setting *out_size.
82	     * Returns 0 on success, nonzero (fatal) on error.
83	     *
84	     * This is the canonical entry point; every spec implements it.
85	     * The dispatch layer falls back to it when the specialised
86	     * paths below are not set. */
87	    lang_invoke_fn invoke;
88	
89	    /* invoke_native — optional fast path used when every wire
90	     * adjacent to this box (input and output) stays inside this
91	     * spec's language. Bytes on the wire are this language's
92	     * native serialisation (Lua tables via msgpack, C struct
93	     * memcpy, etc.) and the encode/decode round trip is skipped.
94	     * NULL means the spec hasn't specialised; dispatch falls
95	     * back to `invoke`. Issue 312. */
96	    lang_invoke_fn invoke_native;
97	
98	    /* invoke_json — optional universal-interop path. Bytes on the
99	     * wire are JSON. NULL means the spec hasn't specialised;
100	     * dispatch falls back to `invoke`. Issue 312. */
101	    lang_invoke_fn invoke_json;
102	
103	    /* Bridges between native and JSON wire forms. Used when a
104	     * value crosses a language boundary — the producer writes
105	     * native, the dispatch layer converts via the producer's
106	     * native_to_json, hands the JSON bytes to the consumer's
107	     * json_to_native. NULL means the spec hasn't specialised;
108	     * dispatch treats native and JSON as identical bytes. Issue
109	     * 312. */
110	    lang_bridge_fn native_to_json;
111	    lang_bridge_fn json_to_native;
112	} lang_spec_t;
113	/* }}} */
114	
115	#ifdef __cplusplus
116	}
117	#endif
118	
119	#endif /* SORAMECH_LANG_SPEC_H */
120	
```

Whether the\n// language *accepts* extra positional args at call time is not a\n// per-function property and not the parser's job; that's declared\n// once per language in `langs/<name>/spec.js` via `variadic_shape`.\n// The function's signature still drives the displayed port names so\n// the user can see the arity the author wrote.\nexport function parse_functions(content) {\n  const fns   = [];\n  const fn_re = /^function\\s+M\\.(\\w+)\\s*\\(([^)]*)\\)/mg;\n  let m;\n  while ((m = fn_re.exec(content)) !== null) {\n    const body_start = m.index + m[0].length;\n    const body_end   = lua_fn_end(content, body_start);\n    const body       = content.slice(body_start, body_end);\n    const raw_params = m[2].trim()\n      ? m[2].split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean)\n      : [];\n    const inputs  = raw_params.filter(p => p !== '...');\n    const outputs = parse_returns(body);\n    fns.push({ name: m[1], inputs, outputs });\n  }\n  return fns;\n}\n// }}}",
  "file_path":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/langs/lua/parser.js",
  "old_string":"// {{{ parse_functions\n// Returns [{name, inputs, outputs, variadic_tail}] for every\n// `function M.<name>(<params>)` declared in the file.\n//\n// The `variadic_tail` field is the addition from issue 217 part C\n// (reopened): true when the parameter list ends with `...`, meaning\n// the function accepts more positional args than its declared params.\n// The trailing `...` is stripped from `inputs` so the editor's port\n// list reflects only the named parameters; the editor uses\n// `variadic_tail` to decide whether marking a port variadic is\n// actually supported by the source.\nexport function parse_functions(content) {\n  const fns   = [];\n  const fn_re = /^function\\s+M\\.(\\w+)\\s*\\(([^)]*)\\)/mg;\n  let m;\n  while ((m = fn_re.exec(content)) !== null) {\n    const body_start = m.index + m[0].length;\n    const body_end   = lua_fn_end(content, body_start);\n    const body       = content.slice(body_start, body_end);\n    const raw_params = m[2].trim()\n      ? m[2].split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean)\n      : [];\n    const variadic_tail = raw_params[raw_params.length - 1] === '...';\n    const inputs        = variadic_tail ? raw_params.slice(0, -1) : raw_params;\n    const outputs       = parse_returns(body);\n    fns.push({ name: m[1], inputs, outputs, variadic_tail });\n  }\n  return fns;\n}\n// }}}",
