{
  "new_string":"## Allocation strategy: one unified allocator for every value\n\nThere is one allocator. It serves both values whose size is known\nfrom the graph at compile time and values whose size only becomes\nknown when they're produced (LLM responses, dynamic arrays, anything\nwhere the upper bound isn't a useful number). The earlier draft of\nthis document split these into two stores — a size-class slot\nallocator for the known case, a separate \"large-value heap\" for the\nunknown case. That split was a mistake. They are the same problem\nand they reuse the same machinery; treating them as two stores led\nto the unknown-size path being a monotonically-growing pool that\nnever reclaims, which is a memory leak by design.\n\n### How the allocator learns sizes ahead of time\n\nBefore the run starts, the loader walks every box in the graph and\nreads the size that box declares for its output. A box that always\nproduces a 32-byte number declares 32. A box that always produces a\n4-megabyte image declares 4 megabytes. The loader collects every\ndistinct declared size, deduplicates them, and creates one free-list\nper distinct size. Each list is pre-populated with enough chunks to\ncover the static demand for that size.\n\nBoxes whose output size depends on runtime — the LLM case — declare\n\"size unknown.\" The loader doesn't create a pre-warmed list for those\nboxes; it knows their values will be allocated on demand.\n\nBoth kinds share the same underlying memory region. The difference is\nonly whether the loader had a list pre-warmed for the value's size.\n\n### Two doors, one allocator\n\n**Door 1 — declared-size allocation.** Given a request for size N,\nwhere N matches a declared size class: pop the head of that class's\nfree-list. One step. This is the common path for the vast majority\nof values; nearly every box in a typical graph has a declared output\nsize.\n\n**Door 2 — runtime-size allocation.** Given a request for an unknown\nsize at runtime: find the smallest size class whose chunks are large\nenough. If that class is exhausted, try the next-larger. If the\nchunk handed out is oversized for the request, slice the unused tail\ninto a residual chunk and push that residual onto the appropriate\nsmaller free-list. The residual is only kept if it's at least as large\nas the smallest declared size class — anything smaller is left as\ninternal waste rather than fragmenting the lists with stubs.\n\nBoth doors fall through to the same fragmentation-recovery and growth\nmachinery below when their free-lists can't satisfy a request.\n\n### Reference counting drives deallocation\n\nEvery value the allocator hands out is reference-counted. The dispatch\nlayer increments the count when handing a value to a consumer and\ndecrements it when the consumer is done. When the count reaches zero,\nthe chunk is returned to the allocator.\n\nThe return-to-allocator step does two things atomically: pushes the\nchunk onto the appropriate free-list AND checks the chunk's two\nphysical neighbors. If either neighbor is also free, the deallocator\nmerges them into a larger chunk and pushes the merged chunk onto the\nfree-list for the merged size. This eager neighbor-merge is O(1) and\nkeeps small-scale fragmentation from accumulating on the hot path.\n\nReference counting requires touching every place that hands out a\npointer to an allocator-managed value. That cost is paid once. After\nthe wiring exists, both doors above benefit from the same recycling.\n\n### When does the bigger sweep run\n\nThe eager neighbor-merge above catches the common case. It misses\npatterns where free chunks are scattered with allocated chunks between\nthem — the cheap merge can't see past its immediate neighbors.\n\nA deeper sweep that walks the whole address-ordered chunk list\nlooking for runs of contiguous free chunks runs on two deterministic\ntriggers, both decoupled from wall-clock time:\n\n1. **At the moment an allocation would otherwise fail.** When a\n   request can't be satisfied from any free-list — including after\n   trying larger classes and slicing — the system runs the deep sweep\n   before falling back to asking the operating system for more memory.\n   If the sweep produces a chunk large enough to satisfy the request,\n   the allocation succeeds and no growth happens. This is the\n   mandatory trigger: the sweep is the immediate alternative to\n   monotonic growth, and it fires exactly when its work has value.\n\n2. **At quiescence — moments when no task is running.** The dispatch\n   layer already recognizes these moments because it uses them to\n   decide a run is complete. Between batches is a natural opportunity\n   because no tasks are reading or writing chunks at that instant.\n   This is the opportunistic trigger.\n\nThe mandatory trigger is enough on its own. The opportunistic trigger\nis a tunable knob that can be enabled later if profiling shows\nfragmentation surviving the cheap merge faster than the mandatory\ntrigger catches up.\n\nA third option was considered — trigger when the count of small free\nfragments crosses a threshold — but rejected for now. It works, but\nit needs the threshold tuned, and the two triggers above cover the\nsame ground without that tuning step.\n\n### Physical-order chunk list (for the merge steps)\n\nBoth the eager neighbor-merge and the deeper sweep need to know\nwhich chunks are physically adjacent in memory. The size-class\nfree-lists don't carry that information — they're indexed by size,\nnot address.\n\nThe supporting structure is a doubly-linked list of all chunks (free\nand allocated) in address order. Splits and merges are O(1) given a\nchunk pointer. Neighbor lookup is O(1). The cost is pointer-chasing\nduring the deeper sweep, but the sweep only runs at the two triggers\nabove, not on the hot path.\n\nIf profiling later shows traversal cost hurting at sweep time, the\nlist can be swapped for a sorted-by-address array of `(start, end)`\npairs with an auxiliary index — the allocator's external API doesn't\nchange.\n\n### Why one allocator instead of two\n\nThe split design existed because the original two-tier scheme had a\n\"large-value heap\" carved out for variable-size payloads, on the\ntheory that mixing variable and fixed sizes in the same pool would\ncomplicate the fixed-size path. That reasoning doesn't survive\ncontact with reference counting and free-list recycling: with\nrefcount-driven free, the fixed-size path stays fast because most\nrequests match a declared class and pop in one step regardless of\nwhat else is in the region.\n\nSplitting them also meant the variable-size pool had to grow\nforever, because writing the same allocator twice — once with\nreclamation, once without — was more work than was budgeted in early\nphase 3. That's the position the current code is in. Unified gets us\nthe reclamation everywhere, with no extra design work.\n\n### Growth (last resort)\n\nWhen both doors fail and the deep sweep can't produce a fitting\nchunk, the allocator grows by asking the operating system for a new\nregion, dividing it into chunks per the declared size classes (or\ninto a single large chunk if the request was for an unknown size\nbeyond all declared classes), and threading the new chunks into the\nfree-lists and the address-ordered list.\n\nGrowth is the rare case. With pre-warmed free-lists sized to static\ndemand and refcount-driven recycling, a well-behaved graph never\nreaches growth — every allocation either pops a pre-warmed chunk or\nrecycles one whose last reference just dropped.",

The map is\nanalyzed at compile time to enumerate every distinct slot size the\ngraph will need (each call box declares its output slot size; each\nqueued-input ring declares its total size from cell capacity × queue\ndepth). The allocator builds one free list per distinct size class.\n\nVariable-size and unknown-size payloads (a long LLM response, an\narray whose length is not known until runtime) are handled by a\nseparate **large-value heap** described below — not by allocating\nhuge slots up front.\n\n### The lists\nEach size class is an array (or linked list) of free slots of exactly\nthat size. At startup, each list is pre-populated with enough slots\nto cover the static box count for that size. Lists grow during the\nrun if demand exceeds the pre-allocation.\n\nEach list has its own mutex — allocation and deallocation in different\nsize classes never contend with each other.\n\n### Allocation\nGiven a request for size N bytes:\n1. Find the smallest size class with `class_size ≥ N`.\n2. If that class has a free slot, pop it. Done.\n3. Otherwise, try the next larger size class. Repeat.\n4. If a larger class hits, the slot is oversized for the request.\n   Try to slice the unused tail into a residual slot. If the residual\n   is at least as large as the smallest size class, push it onto that\n   class's list. Otherwise skip the slice — oversize the allocation\n   and live with the waste until the slot is freed.\n5. If no size class has a free slot, attempt to coalesce: walk the\n   address-ordered chunk list looking for adjacent free chunks that\n   together meet the requested size. If found, merge them and return\n   the combined chunk.\n6. If coalescing fails, grow the region (see \"Growth\" below) and\n   append the new slots to the appropriate lists. Then try again.\n7. After steps 5 or 6 complete, queue a deferred cleanup task on the\n   pool. Cleanup runs on a worker thread later and walks the\n   chunk list to opportunistically coalesce contiguous free chunks\n   into the largest possible chunks. Cleanup is best-effort — it\n   does not block any other thread. If memory fragments faster than\n   cleanup can keep up, the next allocation grows the heap; that's\n   fine.\n\n### Deallocation\nWhen refcount drops to zero, the slot is pushed onto its size class's\nfree list. The deallocator also performs a cheap eager-coalesce step:\ncheck the slot's two physical neighbors; if either is free, merge\nthem and push the combined chunk onto the appropriate size class.\nThis is O(1) given the doubly-linked chunk list (see below) and keeps\nsmall-scale fragmentation in check without needing the deferred\ncleanup task to run.\n\n### Physical-order chunk list (for coalescing)\nCoalescing needs to know which chunks are physically adjacent in\nmemory. Lookup by binary search is O(log N), but inserts and\n  deletes are O(N) without an additional index. Cache-friendly to\n  scan.\n\nThe linked list is simpler and has the right asymptotic behavior for\nsplits/merges, which dominate. Start there. ts: 2026-05-20T00:45:43.546Z

Tests still green (the change was design-document only; no runtime code touched yet).

**What's in the file now:**

The earlier "Lifetime" section that flatly said there's no reference counting has been rewritten to distinguish two things that were running together — the box's slot record (still lives the whole run, as before) and the values flowing through it (now reference-counted memory chunks served by the unified allocator). That distinction was getting blurred in casual talk; the file now makes it explicit.

The "Allocation strategy" section has been restructured around the one-allocator idea. It now reads as one connected story: how sizes are learned from the graph before the run, the two doors for declared-size versus runtime-size allocation, reference counting as the deallocation signal, the cheap neighbor-merge on every free, and the deeper sweep on the two deterministic triggers (allocation-would-otherwise-fail, and quiescence). The address-ordered chunk list that both merge steps depend on is described after them, where it actually makes sense in the narrative.

The old "Large-value heap (variable-size payloads)" subsection is gone as a separate concept. Its responsibilities — handling values whose size isn't known at compile time — are now described as door 2 of the unified allocator, drawing chunks from the same memory the declared-size path uses. The "why one allocator instead of two" subsection explains why the earlier split was a design mistake, so a future reader who finds the implementation logs talking about two stores understands the trajectory.

At the bottom of the implementation log, a retraction entry explicitly withdraws the "acceptable for current fixtures" framing. It names monotonic growth as a memory leak by design, points at the rewritten sections above, and enumerates the work remaining: replace the bump-allocated variable-size pool with the unified allocator, adopt the same allocator for the small fixed-size path, wire reference counting through the dispatch layer, add the address-ordered chunk list, implement the deeper sweep. It states clearly that this is a design commitment, not a completion notice — the allocator hasn't been built yet, but the design isn't deferred anymore.

**On the two questions you asked, recorded in the file:**

The "how does the loader know sizes" answer lives in the new "How the allocator learns sizes ahead of time" subsection. Short version of what's in the file: known sizes come from each box's output-size declaration in the graph metadata, deduplicated into size classes pre-warmed before the run. Unknown sizes are declared as "size unknown" by the box; the loader doesn't pre-warm a list for them, and they go through door 2 at runtime.

The deterministic-sweep answer lives in the "When does the bigger sweep run" subsection. The mandatory trigger is the moment an allocation would otherwise fail. The opportunistic trigger is quiescence between task batches. The threshold-based trigger is mentioned as a third option and rejected for now (works, but needs tuning, and the first two cover the same ground without it).

Before I dive in, the honest scope: this is a multi-stage piece of work. The design in the file covers a new allocator module, replacing one existing module, modifying another, and wiring reference counting through a third. That's bigger than one focused session.

The responsible sequence:

1. **Build the new allocator as a standalone module, with its own tests.** Doesn't touch any existing systems. Verifiable in isolation.
2. **Switch the variable-size-payload path to use it.** Removes the current bump-allocated chain that's the memory leak. Existing tests at the slot-store boundary should pass unchanged.
3. **Switch the fixed-size value path to use it too.** Currently the slot store calls plain malloc per slot at startup. Now those slots come from the unified allocator's free-lists.
4. **Wire reference counting through the dispatch layer.** Increment when handing a value to a consumer, decrement when the consumer is done. This is the widest change in lines-touched, but each individual change is trivial.
5. **Add the deeper sweep on the two trigger points.** The eager neighbor-merge from stage 1 covers the hot path; the sweep is what catches what neighbor-merge misses.

I'll do stage 1 now and stop there. You'll see the new allocator with its full test surface; the existing system keeps running unchanged. After you've looked at it we can decide on the rest.

1	/* src/015-large-value-heap.h — variable-size payload allocator.
2	 *
3	 * The large-value heap is a chunked arena: a linked list of malloc'd
4	 * chunks where each allocation bump-allocates within the current
5	 * chunk. When a chunk fills up, a new chunk is allocated and prepended.
6	 * Allocations are never individually freed — the entire heap is
7	 * destroyed at run end (same lifetime model as the slot store).
8	 *
9	 * Why a separate heap, not just bigger slot cells:
10	 *
11	 *   The slot store gives every cell a fixed cell_capacity decided at
12	 *   slot_alloc time. For values whose size is not known at compile
13	 *   time — long LLM responses, dynamically-sized arrays, binary
14	 *   blobs of unbounded length — there is no useful upper bound to
15	 *   bake into cell_capacity.
16	 *
17	 *   The large-value heap solves this by decoupling cell size from
18	 *   payload size. A slot allocated with SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE
19	 *   (issue 302) stores only a handle in its cell; the payload bytes
20	 *   live in the large-value heap and the cell carries a stable
21	 *   pointer to them. Push allocates a heap region of the requested
22	 *   size; pop follows the pointer.
23	 *
24	 * Why stable pointers matter: the heap may grow by appending new
25	 * chunks, but existing chunks never move, so a pointer returned by
26	 * lvh_alloc remains valid for the life of the heap. This is what
27	 * makes the design safe for concurrent producers and consumers —
28	 * the consumer's pointer doesn't get invalidated by a later
29	 * producer's allocation.
30	 *
31	 * Concurrency: lvh_alloc is thread-safe via an internal mutex.
32	 * Reads of returned pointers need no synchronization beyond what
33	 * the slot store already provides at the cell level.
34	 *
35	 * Cleanup: allocations are not individually freed. The heap is
36	 * monotonic-growth for the duration of a run. For long-running
37	 * graphs with iterators producing large values in a tight loop,
38	 * this means the heap grows in proportion to total bytes produced
39	 * — not in proportion to peak live bytes. That's a known trade-off;
40	 * a proper free-on-pop allocator is a future iteration. Track
41	 * total_allocated() to surface when it matters.
42	 */
43	
44	#ifndef SORAMECH_LARGE_VALUE_HEAP_H
45	#define SORAMECH_LARGE_VALUE_HEAP_H
46	
47	#include <stddef.h>
48	#include <stdint.h>
49	
50	#ifdef __cplusplus
51	extern "C" {
52	#endif
53

55	typedef struct lvh lvh_t;
56	/* }}} */
57	
58	/* {{{ Lifecycle */
59	/* Construct a new heap.
60	 *
61	 *   default_chunk_size — bytes per new chunk when the heap grows.
62	 *                        Pass 0 to use the built-in default (64 KB).
63	 *
64	 * Returns NULL on allocation failure. */
65	lvh_t  *lvh_create(size_t default_chunk_size);
66	
67	/* Free every chunk and the heap struct. Safe on NULL. After this
68	 * call, all pointers returned by lvh_alloc on this heap are
69	 * invalid. */
70	void    lvh_destroy(lvh_t *h);
71	/* }}} */
72	
73	/* {{{ Allocation */
74	/* Allocate `size` bytes of unspecified contents. The returned
75	 * pointer is stable for the life of the heap. Returns NULL on
76	 * allocation failure (out of memory at the malloc layer; the
77	 * heap itself doesn't pre-cap).
78	 *
79	 * Allocations larger than `default_chunk_size` get a dedicated
80	 * chunk sized exactly to the request — no waste, no fragmentation
81	 * of the regular chunks. Allocations smaller or equal share the
82	 * current bump chunk. */
83	void   *lvh_alloc(lvh_t *h, size_t size);
84	/* }}} */

68	static lvh_chunk_t *alloc_chunk(lvh_t *h, size_t min_cap)
69	{
70	    size_t cap = h->default_chunk_size;
71	    if (min_cap > cap) {
72	        /* Caller asked for more than a default chunk holds. Give it
73	         * its own oversized chunk sized to exactly fit; this keeps

75	         * unusual allocation. */

77	    }

79	    if (!c) return NULL;
80	    c->next = NULL;
81	    c->cap  = cap;
82	    c->used = 0;

89	}

93	lvh_t *lvh_create(size_t default_chunk_size)
94	{
95	    lvh_t *h = calloc(1, sizeof *h);
96	    if (!h) return NULL;
97	    h->default_chunk_size = (default_chunk_size > 0)
98	                            ? default_chunk_size
99	                            : LVH_DEFAULT_CHUNK_SIZE;
100	    if (pthread_mutex_init(&h->mu, NULL) != 0) {
101	        free(h);
102	        return NULL;
103	    }
104	    atomic_init(&h->total_allocated, 0u);
105	    atomic_init(&h->total_used,      0u);
106	    atomic_init(&h->chunk_count,     0u);
107	    return h;
108	}
109	/* }}} */
110	
111	/* {{{ lvh_destroy() */
112	void lvh_destroy(lvh_t *h)
113	{
114	    if (!h) return;
115	    lvh_chunk_t *c = h->head;
116	    while (c) {
117	        lvh_chunk_t *next = c->next;
118	        free(c);
119	        c = next;
120	    }
121	    pthread_mutex_destroy(&h->mu);
122	    free(h);
123	}

128	{
129	    if (!h) return NULL;
130	    if (size == 0) {
131	        /* Zero-byte allocation: return a non-NULL "this is intentional"
132	         * pointer. Hand out a stable address that nobody else will
133	         * see — the head of an allocation of LVH_ALIGN bytes, which

135	         * casing the empty case while still letting NULL mean
136	         * "failure". */

142	    pthread_mutex_lock(&h->mu);

144	    /* Fast path: current chunk has room. */

147	        lvh_chunk_t *fresh = alloc_chunk(h, need);
148	        if (!fresh) {
149	            pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);
150	            return NULL;
151	        }
152	        fresh->next = h->head;
153	        h->head     = fresh;
154	        c           = fresh;
155	    }
156	
157	    void *p = c->data + c->used;
158	    c->used += need;
159	
160	    atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->total_used,
161	                              (uint64_t)need,
162	                              memory_order_relaxed);
163	
164	    pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);
165	    return p;

{
  "content":"/* src/016-unified-allocator.h — the one allocator described in\n * issue 302's \"Allocation strategy\" section.\n *\n * What this module does, in plain terms:\n *\n *   Before the run starts, the loader writes down every distinct\n *   output-size declared by any box in the graph and how many boxes\n *   need each size. ua_create takes that list and pre-builds one\n *   free-list per distinct size, populated with enough chunks to\n *   cover the demand the loader counted.\n *\n *   During the run, ua_alloc(size) finds the smallest pre-built\n *   class whose chunks are large enough and hands one back. If the\n *   class is empty (demand exceeded what the loader pre-warmed) or\n *   the request is larger than any declared class (a runtime-only\n *   size), the allocator grows by getting another region from the\n *   operating system.\n *\n *   Every chunk handed out carries a reference count. ua_ref bumps\n *   it; ua_unref drops it; when the count hits zero, the chunk goes\n *   back to its free-list AND the deallocator checks the chunk's\n *   immediate neighbors in memory. If either neighbor is also free,\n *   they fuse into one larger chunk that goes onto the appropriate\n *   larger free-list. This is the \"eager neighbor merge\" from the\n *   issue file — O(1) and runs on every free.\n *\n *   When ua_alloc can't find a fitting chunk from any free-list,\n *   ua_sweep runs first (walks the address-ordered chunk list\n *   looking for runs of free chunks that the cheap neighbor-merge\n *   couldn't catch, fuses them). If the sweep produces a fitting\n *   chunk, the allocation succeeds without growing. If not, the\n *   heap grows. This is the \"mandatory deep sweep at allocation\n *   failure\" trigger from the issue file.\n *\n *   The quiescence trigger described in the issue file is not in\n *   this stage — the dispatch layer will call ua_sweep at its own\n *   quiescence points when that integration happens. For now the\n *   only automatic sweep is the one inside ua_alloc.\n *\n * What this module does NOT do (yet):\n *   - It does not integrate with the slot store or the dispatch\n *     layer. Those are later stages of the implementation. The\n *     existing chained-block region for variable-size payloads\n *     remains in use until those stages land.\n *   - It does not slice oversized chunks (a 2000-byte request that\n *     pops a 4096-byte chunk does not get the 2096-byte tail\n *     pushed back as a residual). The eager neighbor-merge and the\n *     pre-warmed class sizes cover the common case; slicing is a\n *     future optimization the API supports without source changes.\n *   - It uses one allocator-wide mutex instead of per-class locks.\n *     The issue file describes per-class locks as the optimization\n *     path; this stage keeps the locking simple and correct.\n *\n * Designed in issue 302's rewrite (2026-05-19).\n */\n\n#ifndef SORAMECH_UNIFIED_ALLOCATOR_H\n#define SORAMECH_UNIFIED_ALLOCATOR_H\n\n#include <stddef.h>\n#include <stdint.h>\n\n#ifdef __cplusplus\nextern \"C\" {\n#endif\n\n/* {{{ Opaque types */\ntypedef struct ua       ua_t;        /* the heap */\ntypedef struct ua_chunk ua_chunk_t;  /* one allocation */\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Declared size classes */\n/* A single (size, count) pair telling the allocator: \"the graph\n * needs `count` chunks of `size` bytes.\" */\ntypedef struct ua_class_decl {\n    size_t   size;\n    uint32_t prewarm_count;\n} ua_class_decl_t;\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Lifecycle */\n/* Construct a new heap.\n *\n *   classes        — array of declared size classes from graph analysis,\n *                    or NULL/zero if no pre-warming is wanted. Sizes\n *                    do not need to be sorted; the allocator sorts\n *                    them internally. Duplicate sizes are merged\n *                    (counts summed).\n *   n_classes      — number of entries in `classes`.\n *\n * Returns NULL on allocation failure or invalid arguments (e.g. any\n * class with size == 0). */\nua_t *ua_create(const ua_class_decl_t *classes, size_t n_classes);\n\n/* Free every region and the heap struct. Safe on NULL. After this\n * call, every chunk previously returned by ua_alloc on this heap is\n * invalid. */\nvoid  ua_destroy(ua_t *h);\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Allocation */\n/* Allocate a chunk of at least `size` bytes. The returned chunk has\n * reference count 1; the caller is the initial owner.\n *\n * Lookup is \"smallest free-list whose class size >= requested.\" If\n * no free-list has a chunk and the sweep can't produce one, the\n * heap grows by getting a new region from the OS.\n *\n * Returns NULL on allocation failure or if h is NULL or size is 0. */\nua_chunk_t *ua_alloc(ua_t *h, size_t size);\n\n/* Bump the chunk's reference count by 1. Safe on NULL chunk\n * (no-op). Returns the chunk for chaining. */\nua_chunk_t *ua_ref(ua_chunk_t *chunk);\n\n/* Drop the chunk's reference count by 1. If the count reaches zero\n * the chunk is returned to its free-list and an O(1) eager merge\n * with physical neighbors runs. Safe on NULL chunk (no-op). */\nvoid ua_unref(ua_t *h, ua_chunk_t *chunk);\n\n/* Direct accessors. Both safe on NULL (data returns NULL, size\n * returns 0). */\nvoid   *ua_data(ua_chunk_t *chunk);\nsize_t  ua_size(ua_chunk_t *chunk);\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Sweep */\n/* Walk the address-ordered chunk list and fuse runs of contiguous\n * free chunks that the eager neighbor-merge couldn't catch (e.g.\n * because the middle chunk in a 1-2-3 free run only got merged with\n * one neighbor at unref time, leaving the other free chunk on a\n * different list).\n *\n * The allocator runs this automatically before growing the heap.\n * Callers may also invoke it directly at quiescence points (e.g.\n * between dispatch batches). Returns the number of merges\n * performed. */\nuint32_t ua_sweep(ua_t *h);\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Stats (test / debug / future telemetry) */\nuint64_t ua_bytes_in_use(const ua_t *h);\nuint64_t ua_bytes_free  (const ua_t *h);\nuint64_t ua_bytes_total (const ua_t *h);  /* sum of every region's capacity */\nuint32_t ua_region_count(const ua_t *h);\nuint32_t ua_alloc_calls (const ua_t *h);\nuint32_t ua_free_calls  (const ua_t *h);  /* unrefs that reached zero */\nuint32_t ua_neighbor_merges(const ua_t *h);\nuint32_t ua_sweep_merges(const ua_t *h);\nuint32_t ua_grow_calls  (const ua_t *h);\n\n/* For tests: how many free chunks currently sit on the free-list\n * for the class whose chunks are exactly `size` bytes. Returns 0\n * if no such class exists or the list is empty. */\nuint32_t ua_class_free_count(const ua_t *h, size_t size);\n/* }}} */\n\n#ifdef __cplusplus\n}\n#endif\n\n#endif /* SORAMECH_UNIFIED_ALLOCATOR_H */\n",

{
  "content":"/* src/016-unified-allocator.c — implementation of the unified\n * allocator declared in 016-unified-allocator.h.\n *\n * The story this file tells, in order:\n *\n *   A chunk header carries the chunk's size, refcount, free flag,\n *   and three pointers: two for the address-ordered doubly-linked\n *   list (so neighbor-merge can find what's physically next to it),\n *   and one for the free-list it currently sits on (when free).\n *   The payload bytes follow the header as a flexible array.\n *\n *   The allocator owns a sorted array of size classes. For each\n *   class there's a head pointer into a singly-linked free-list of\n *   chunks of exactly that size.\n *\n *   The allocator also owns a list of \"regions\" — single mallocs\n *   from the OS, each carved into chunks at creation time. Every\n *   chunk in a region is in the region's own address-ordered list\n *   (so the prev/next pointers actually reflect physical\n *   adjacency). Region boundaries are non-mergeable.\n *\n *   ua_alloc(size) finds the smallest class whose size >= request,\n *   pops the head of that class's free-list, marks the chunk\n *   allocated (refcount=1, is_free=0), and returns it.\n *\n *   If the class is empty, the allocator tries larger classes.\n *   If every class is empty, the allocator runs the sweep, then\n *   tries again. If still nothing, it grows the heap by allocating\n *   a new region containing one chunk of the requested size (with\n *   the request rounded up to the requested class size if it\n *   matched a declared class, or aligned up if it didn't).\n *\n *   ua_unref drops the refcount. When it reaches zero, the chunk\n *   is pushed onto its class's free-list, then the deallocator\n *   checks the chunk's addr_prev and addr_next. If either neighbor\n *   is free, the two chunks are unlinked from their current\n *   free-lists, fused (the lower chunk's size absorbs the higher\n *   chunk; the higher chunk's address-list slot is removed), and\n *   the merged chunk is pushed onto the appropriate free-list. The\n *   merged size might match an existing class (push onto that\n *   class's list) or it might not (push onto the smallest class\n *   whose size >= merged_size, which is acceptable because alloc\n *   only ever looks at the chunk's actual size, not its class).\n *\n * One mutex on the whole allocator. Per-class locking is the\n * documented next step but not in this stage.\n *\n * Designed in issue 302's rewrite (2026-05-19).\n */\n\n#include \"016-unified-allocator.h\"\n\n#include <pthread.h>\n#include <stdatomic.h>\n#include <stdlib.h>\n#include <string.h>\n\n/* {{{ Alignment */\n/* Payload alignment — 16 bytes is sufficient for any standard C\n * scalar or SIMD vector. Chunk headers are sized so the flexible\n * array begins on this boundary. */\n#define UA_ALIGN 16u\n\nstatic inline size_t align_up(size_t n, size_t a)\n{\n    return (n + (a - 1u)) & ~(a - 1u);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Chunk header */\nstruct ua_chunk {\n    /* Address-ordered doubly-linked list, scoped to the chunk's\n     * region. NULL at region boundaries — never link across regions. */\n    struct ua_chunk *addr_prev;\n    struct ua_chunk *addr_next;\n\n    /* Singly-linked free-list pointer. Only meaningful when\n     * is_free == 1. */\n    struct ua_chunk *free_next;\n\n    /* Bytes available to the caller (does not count this header). */\n    size_t size;\n\n    /* The class this chunk's free-list belongs to. Set at chunk\n     * creation; updated when an eager merge promotes the chunk to\n     * a different list. */\n    size_t class_size;\n\n    /* Reference count. Atomic for ua_ref/ua_unref under non-locked\n     * paths in future stages; mutex covers the structural operations\n     * (free-list manipulation, neighbor merging) where atomicity of\n     * the count alone is not sufficient. */\n    _Atomic uint32_t refcount;\n\n    uint8_t is_free;\n    uint8_t _pad[7];\n\n    /* Flexible-array payload. Header is sized so this begins on a\n     * UA_ALIGN boundary on every platform we target. */\n    uint8_t data[];\n};\n\n#define UA_HEADER_BYTES sizeof(struct ua_chunk)\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Heap struct */\nstruct ua {\n    pthread_mutex_t mu;\n\n    /* Size classes, sorted ascending. */\n    size_t       *class_sizes;\n    ua_chunk_t  **class_heads;     /* free-list head per class */\n    size_t        n_classes;\n    size_t        classes_cap;\n\n    /* Regions. Each entry is the base pointer of one big malloc. */\n    void        **regions;\n    ua_chunk_t  **region_firsts;   /* first chunk in each region */\n    size_t        n_regions;\n    size_t        regions_cap;\n\n    /* Stats. */\n    _Atomic uint64_t bytes_in_use;\n    _Atomic uint64_t bytes_free;\n    _Atomic uint64_t bytes_total;\n    _Atomic uint32_t alloc_calls;\n    _Atomic uint32_t free_calls;\n    _Atomic uint32_t neighbor_merges;\n    _Atomic uint32_t sweep_merges;\n    _Atomic uint32_t grow_calls;\n};\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Local helper — find_class_index */\n/* Smallest class index whose class_sizes[i] >= want. Returns\n * h->n_classes if none. Caller holds h->mu. */\nstatic size_t find_class_index(const ua_t *h, size_t want)\n{\n    /* Linear scan: class counts are tiny in practice (the graph\n     * declares a handful of distinct sizes). Binary search becomes\n     * worthwhile only past a few dozen classes; not the case here. */\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < h->n_classes; i++) {\n        if (h->class_sizes[i] >= want) return i;\n    }\n    return h->n_classes;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Local helper — ensure_class */\n/* Make sure a class with this exact size exists; return its index.\n * If the class is new, the new free-list head is NULL. Caller\n * holds h->mu. Returns SIZE_MAX on allocation failure. */\nstatic size_t ensure_class(ua_t *h, size_t size)\n{\n    /* Already present? */\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < h->n_classes; i++) {\n        if (h->class_sizes[i] == size) return i;\n    }\n\n    /* Grow the arrays if needed. */\n    if (h->n_classes == h->classes_cap) {\n        size_t new_cap = h->classes_cap ? h->classes_cap * 2 : 8;\n        size_t      *new_sizes = realloc(h->class_sizes, new_cap * sizeof *new_sizes);\n        if (!new_sizes) return (size_t)-1;\n        h->class_sizes = new_sizes;\n        ua_chunk_t **new_heads = realloc(h->class_heads, new_cap * sizeof *new_heads);\n        if (!new_heads) return (size_t)-1;\n        h->class_heads = new_heads;\n        h->classes_cap = new_cap;\n    }\n\n    /* Insert sorted. */\n    size_t pos = h->n_classes;\n    while (pos > 0 && h->class_sizes[pos - 1] > size) pos--;\n    if (pos < h->n_classes) {\n        memmove(&h->class_sizes[pos + 1], &h->class_sizes[pos],\n                (h->n_classes - pos) * sizeof *h->class_sizes);\n        memmove(&h->class_heads[pos + 1], &h->class_heads[pos],\n                (h->n_classes - pos) * sizeof *h->class_heads);\n    }\n    h->class_sizes[pos] = size;\n    h->class_heads[pos] = NULL;\n    h->n_classes++;\n    return pos;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Local helper — push_to_free_list */\n/* Push `c` onto the free-list for `class_idx`. Caller holds h->mu. */\nstatic void push_to_free_list(ua_t *h, size_t class_idx, ua_chunk_t *c)\n{\n    c->is_free    = 1;\n    c->class_size = h->class_sizes[class_idx];\n    c->free_next  = h->class_heads[class_idx];\n    h->class_heads[class_idx] = c;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Local helper — remove_from_free_list */\n/* Unlink `c` from its current free-list. Caller holds h->mu. */\nstatic void remove_from_free_list(ua_t *h, ua_chunk_t *c)\n{\n    size_t idx = (size_t)-1;\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < h->n_classes; i++) {\n        if (h->class_sizes[i] == c->class_size) {\n            idx = i;\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n    if (idx == (size_t)-1) return;  /* shouldn't happen but defensive */\n\n    ua_chunk_t **link = &h->class_heads[idx];\n    while (*link && *link != c) link = &(*link)->free_next;\n    if (*link == c) *link = c->free_next;\n    c->free_next = NULL;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Local helper — register_region */\n/* Append `base` and `first` to the region tracking arrays. Caller\n * holds h->mu. Returns 0 on success, -1 on allocation failure. */\nstatic int register_region(ua_t *h, void *base, ua_chunk_t *first)\n{\n    if (h->n_regions == h->regions_cap) {\n        size_t new_cap = h->regions_cap ? h->regions_cap * 2 : 4;\n        void       **nr = realloc(h->regions,        new_cap * sizeof *nr);\n        if (!nr) return -1;\n        h->regions      = nr;\n        ua_chunk_t **nf = realloc(h->region_firsts,  new_cap * sizeof *nf);\n        if (!nf) return -1;\n        h->region_firsts = nf;\n        h->regions_cap   = new_cap;\n    }\n    h->regions[h->n_regions]       = base;\n    h->region_firsts[h->n_regions] = first;\n    h->n_regions++;\n    return 0;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Local helper — carve_region */\n/* Build a region containing chunks per the given (size, count)\n * pairs, in declaration order. Pushes every chunk onto its class's\n * free-list and links them in address order. Caller holds h->mu.\n *\n * Returns 0 on success, -1 on allocation failure. */\nstatic int carve_region(ua_t *h,\n                        const ua_class_decl_t *classes,\n                        size_t n_classes)\n{\n    size_t total = 0;\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n_classes; i++) {\n        size_t s = align_up(classes[i].size, UA_ALIGN);\n        total += (UA_HEADER_BYTES + s) * classes[i].prewarm_count;\n    }\n    if (total == 0) return 0;\n\n    /* One malloc, generously aligned. */\n    void *base = aligned_alloc(UA_ALIGN, align_up(total, UA_ALIGN));\n    if (!base) return -1;\n\n    /* Carve chunks back-to-back. */\n    uint8_t    *cursor = base;\n    ua_chunk_t *first  = NULL;\n    ua_chunk_t *prev   = NULL;\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n_classes; i++) {\n        size_t s = align_up(classes[i].size, UA_ALIGN);\n        size_t class_idx = ensure_class(h, s);\n        if (class_idx == (size_t)-1) {\n            free(base);\n            return -1;\n        }\n        for (uint32_t k = 0; k < classes[i].prewarm_count; k++) {\n            ua_chunk_t *c = (ua_chunk_t *)cursor;\n            c->addr_prev = prev;\n            c->addr_next = NULL;\n            c->free_next = NULL;\n            c->size      = s;\n            atomic_store_explicit(&c->refcount, 0, memory_order_relaxed);\n            if (prev) prev->addr_next = c;\n            if (!first) first = c;\n            push_to_free_list(h, class_idx, c);\n            prev = c;\n            cursor += UA_HEADER_BYTES + s;\n            atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->bytes_free, (uint64_t)s,\n                                      memory_order_relaxed);\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (register_region(h, base, first) < 0) {\n        free(base);\n        return -1;\n    }\n    atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->bytes_total, (uint64_t)total,\n                              memory_order_relaxed);\n    return 0;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ ua_create */\nua_t *ua_create(const ua_class_decl_t *classes, size_t n_classes)\n{\n    /* Validate. */\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n_classes; i++) {\n        if (classes[i].size == 0) return NULL;\n    }\n\n    ua_t *h = calloc(1, sizeof *h);\n    if (!h) return NULL;\n    if (pthread_mutex_init(&h->mu, NULL) != 0) {\n        free(h);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    /* De-duplicate by summing counts for identical sizes. */\n    if (n_classes > 0) {\n        ua_class_decl_t *dedup = calloc(n_classes, sizeof *dedup);\n        if (!dedup) {\n            pthread_mutex_destroy(&h->mu);\n            free(h);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n        size_t k = 0;\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < n_classes; i++) {\n            size_t aligned = align_up(classes[i].size, UA_ALIGN);\n            size_t j;\n            for (j = 0; j < k; j++) {\n                if (dedup[j].size == aligned) {\n                    dedup[j].prewarm_count += classes[i].prewarm_count;\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n            if (j == k) {\n                dedup[k].size = aligned;\n                dedup[k].prewarm_count = classes[i].prewarm_count;\n                k++;\n            }\n        }\n        if (carve_region(h, dedup, k) < 0) {\n            free(dedup);\n            ua_destroy(h);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n        free(dedup);\n    }\n\n    return h;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ ua_destroy */\nvoid ua_destroy(ua_t *h)\n{\n    if (!h) return;\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < h->n_regions; i++) free(h->regions[i]);\n    free(h->regions);\n    free(h->region_firsts);\n    free(h->class_sizes);\n    free(h->class_heads);\n    pthread_mutex_destroy(&h->mu);\n    free(h);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Local helper — grow_for_size */\n/* Add a new region containing one chunk that satisfies `want_size`.\n * The chunk is left on the free-list of the appropriate class.\n * Caller holds h->mu. Returns 0 on success, -1 on allocation\n * failure. */\nstatic int grow_for_size(ua_t *h, size_t want_size)\n{\n    size_t s = align_up(want_size, UA_ALIGN);\n    ua_class_decl_t one = { .size = s, .prewarm_count = 1 };\n    int rc = carve_region(h, &one, 1);\n    if (rc == 0) {\n        atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->grow_calls, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n    }\n    return rc;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Local helper — eager_merge */\n/* If either neighbor of `c` (which has just been pushed onto its\n * free-list) is free, fuse them into one larger chunk. Updates\n * stats. Caller holds h->mu. */\nstatic void eager_merge(ua_t *h, ua_chunk_t *c)\n{\n    /* Walk forward first: while addr_next is free, absorb it. */\n    while (c->addr_next && c->addr_next->is_free) {\n        ua_chunk_t *n = c->addr_next;\n\n        remove_from_free_list(h, c);\n        remove_from_free_list(h, n);\n\n        /* Fuse: c grows to cover the header + payload of n. */\n        c->size += UA_HEADER_BYTES + n->size;\n        c->addr_next = n->addr_next;\n        if (n->addr_next) n->addr_next->addr_prev = c;\n\n        size_t new_class = ensure_class(h, c->size);\n        if (new_class == (size_t)-1) {\n            /* Couldn't grow class array. Restore c onto a list that\n             * already exists — find any class with size matching or\n             * larger and put c there. Should never happen in practice. */\n            push_to_free_list(h, 0, c);\n            return;\n        }\n        push_to_free_list(h, new_class, c);\n        atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->neighbor_merges, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n    }\n\n    /* Then walk backward: while addr_prev is free, prev absorbs c. */\n    while (c->addr_prev && c->addr_prev->is_free) {\n        ua_chunk_t *p = c->addr_prev;\n\n        remove_from_free_list(h, c);\n        remove_from_free_list(h, p);\n\n        p->size += UA_HEADER_BYTES + c->size;\n        p->addr_next = c->addr_next;\n        if (c->addr_next) c->addr_next->addr_prev = p;\n\n        size_t new_class = ensure_class(h, p->size);\n        if (new_class == (size_t)-1) {\n            push_to_free_list(h, 0, p);\n            return;\n        }\n        push_to_free_list(h, new_class, p);\n        atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->neighbor_merges, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n        c = p;  /* continue from the merged chunk */\n    }\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ ua_alloc */\nua_chunk_t *ua_alloc(ua_t *h, size_t size)\n{\n    if (!h || size == 0) return NULL;\n    size_t aligned = align_up(size, UA_ALIGN);\n\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&h->mu);\n\n    for (int attempt = 0; attempt < 3; attempt++) {\n        size_t idx = find_class_index(h, aligned);\n        if (idx < h->n_classes && h->class_heads[idx]) {\n            ua_chunk_t *c = h->class_heads[idx];\n            h->class_heads[idx] = c->free_next;\n            c->free_next = NULL;\n            c->is_free   = 0;\n            atomic_store_explicit(&c->refcount, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n            atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->alloc_calls, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n            atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->bytes_in_use, (uint64_t)c->size,\n                                      memory_order_relaxed);\n            atomic_fetch_sub_explicit(&h->bytes_free, (uint64_t)c->size,\n                                      memory_order_relaxed);\n            pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);\n            return c;\n        }\n        /* Try larger classes. */\n        size_t larger = idx + 1;\n        while (larger < h->n_classes && !h->class_heads[larger]) larger++;\n        if (larger < h->n_classes) {\n            ua_chunk_t *c = h->class_heads[larger];\n            h->class_heads[larger] = c->free_next;\n            c->free_next = NULL;\n            c->is_free   = 0;\n            atomic_store_explicit(&c->refcount, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n            atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->alloc_calls, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n            atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->bytes_in_use, (uint64_t)c->size,\n                                      memory_order_relaxed);\n            atomic_fetch_sub_explicit(&h->bytes_free, (uint64_t)c->size,\n                                      memory_order_relaxed);\n            pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);\n            return c;\n        }\n\n        if (attempt == 0) {\n            /* Mandatory sweep before growing. */\n            uint32_t merges = 0;\n            for (size_t r = 0; r < h->n_regions; r++) {\n                ua_chunk_t *cur = h->region_firsts[r];\n                while (cur && cur->addr_next) {\n                    if (cur->is_free && cur->addr_next->is_free) {\n                        ua_chunk_t *n = cur->addr_next;\n                        remove_from_free_list(h, cur);\n                        remove_from_free_list(h, n);\n                        cur->size += UA_HEADER_BYTES + n->size;\n                        cur->addr_next = n->addr_next;\n                        if (n->addr_next) n->addr_next->addr_prev = cur;\n                        size_t new_class = ensure_class(h, cur->size);\n                        if (new_class != (size_t)-1) {\n                            push_to_free_list(h, new_class, cur);\n                        }\n                        merges++;\n                        continue;  /* re-check from cur — might fuse further */\n                    }\n                    cur = cur->addr_next;\n                }\n            }\n            atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->sweep_merges, merges, memory_order_relaxed);\n            continue;  /* retry from the top */\n        }\n\n        if (attempt == 1) {\n            /* Grow. */\n            if (grow_for_size(h, aligned) < 0) {\n                pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);\n                return NULL;\n            }\n            continue;  /* retry from the top — should succeed now */\n        }\n    }\n\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);\n    return NULL;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ ua_ref */\nua_chunk_t *ua_ref(ua_chunk_t *chunk)\n{\n    if (!chunk) return NULL;\n    atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&chunk->refcount, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n    return chunk;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ ua_unref */\nvoid ua_unref(ua_t *h, ua_chunk_t *chunk)\n{\n    if (!h || !chunk) return;\n    uint32_t prev = atomic_fetch_sub_explicit(&chunk->refcount, 1u,\n                                              memory_order_acq_rel);\n    if (prev != 1) return;  /* still has references */\n\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&h->mu);\n\n    /* It is possible (under future per-class locking) that another\n     * thread ref'd between the atomic decrement and acquiring the\n     * mutex. Re-check. */\n    if (atomic_load_explicit(&chunk->refcount, memory_order_acquire) > 0) {\n        pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);\n        return;\n    }\n\n    atomic_fetch_sub_explicit(&h->bytes_in_use, (uint64_t)chunk->size,\n                              memory_order_relaxed);\n    atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->bytes_free, (uint64_t)chunk->size,\n                              memory_order_relaxed);\n    atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->free_calls, 1u, memory_order_relaxed);\n\n    size_t class_idx = ensure_class(h, chunk->size);\n    if (class_idx != (size_t)-1) {\n        push_to_free_list(h, class_idx, chunk);\n        eager_merge(h, chunk);\n    }\n\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ ua_data, ua_size */\nvoid *ua_data(ua_chunk_t *chunk)\n{\n    if (!chunk) return NULL;\n    return chunk->data;\n}\n\nsize_t ua_size(ua_chunk_t *chunk)\n{\n    if (!chunk) return 0;\n    return chunk->size;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ ua_sweep */\nuint32_t ua_sweep(ua_t *h)\n{\n    if (!h) return 0;\n    uint32_t merges = 0;\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&h->mu);\n    for (size_t r = 0; r < h->n_regions; r++) {\n        ua_chunk_t *cur = h->region_firsts[r];\n        while (cur && cur->addr_next) {\n            if (cur->is_free && cur->addr_next->is_free) {\n                ua_chunk_t *n = cur->addr_next;\n                remove_from_free_list(h, cur);\n                remove_from_free_list(h, n);\n                cur->size += UA_HEADER_BYTES + n->size;\n                cur->addr_next = n->addr_next;\n                if (n->addr_next) n->addr_next->addr_prev = cur;\n                size_t new_class = ensure_class(h, cur->size);\n                if (new_class != (size_t)-1) {\n                    push_to_free_list(h, new_class, cur);\n                }\n                merges++;\n                continue;\n            }\n            cur = cur->addr_next;\n        }\n    }\n    atomic_fetch_add_explicit(&h->sweep_merges, merges, memory_order_relaxed);\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&h->mu);\n    return merges;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Stats accessors */\nuint64_t ua_bytes_in_use(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? atomic_load_explicit(&h->bytes_in_use, memory_order_relaxed) : 0;\n}\nuint64_t ua_bytes_free(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? atomic_load_explicit(&h->bytes_free, memory_order_relaxed) : 0;\n}\nuint64_t ua_bytes_total(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? atomic_load_explicit(&h->bytes_total, memory_order_relaxed) : 0;\n}\nuint32_t ua_region_count(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? (uint32_t)h->n_regions : 0;\n}\nuint32_t ua_alloc_calls(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? atomic_load_explicit(&h->alloc_calls, memory_order_relaxed) : 0;\n}\nuint32_t ua_free_calls(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? atomic_load_explicit(&h->free_calls, memory_order_relaxed) : 0;\n}\nuint32_t ua_neighbor_merges(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? atomic_load_explicit(&h->neighbor_merges, memory_order_relaxed) : 0;\n}\nuint32_t ua_sweep_merges(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? atomic_load_explicit(&h->sweep_merges, memory_order_relaxed) : 0;\n}\nuint32_t ua_grow_calls(const ua_t *h)\n{\n    return h ? atomic_load_explicit(&h->grow_calls, memory_order_relaxed) : 0;\n}\nuint32_t ua_class_free_count(const ua_t *h, size_t size)\n{\n    if (!h) return 0;\n    size_t target = align_up(size, UA_ALIGN);\n    uint32_t count = 0;\n    pthread_mutex_lock((pthread_mutex_t *)&h->mu);\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < h->n_classes; i++) {\n        if (h->class_sizes[i] != target) continue;\n        for (ua_chunk_t *c = h->class_heads[i]; c; c = c->free_next) count++;\n        break;\n    }\n    pthread_mutex_unlock((pthread_mutex_t *)&h->mu);\n    return count;\n}\n/* }}} */\n",

Each test is independent (its\n * own heap), so failures isolate cleanly.\n *\n * What's covered:\n *   - empty-heap create/destroy round-trip\n *   - pre-warming: declared sizes produce ready-to-pop chunks\n *   - alloc + unref reuses chunks (no growth)\n *   - reference counting: extra ref keeps chunk live\n *   - adjacent free chunks merge eagerly on the second unref\n *   - non-adjacent frees stay separate\n *   - alloc on empty class falls through to a larger class\n *   - alloc above all declared sizes triggers growth\n *   - sweep merges runs of contiguous free chunks\n *   - duplicate sizes in the declaration get summed\n *   - 8-thread concurrent alloc/unref stress\n *\n * Designed in issue 302's rewrite; first stage of the unified\n * allocator implementation.\n */\n\n#include \"../src/016-unified-allocator.h\"\n\n#include <pthread.h>\n#include <stdatomic.h>\n#include <stdio.h>\n#include <stdlib.h>\n#include <string.h>\n\n/* {{{ Test harness */\nstatic int pass = 0;\nstatic int fail = 0;\n\n#define CHECK(cond, msg, ...) do {                                      \\\n    if (!(cond)) {                                                      \\\n        fprintf(stderr, \"  FAIL: \" msg \" (%s:%d)\\n\",                    \\\n                ##__VA_ARGS__, __FILE__, __LINE__);                     \\\n        fail++;                                                         \\\n        return;                                                         \\\n    }                                                                   \\\n} while (0)\n\n#define PASS(name) do { pass++; printf(\"  ok   %s\\n\", name); } while (0)\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_empty_heap */\nstatic void test_empty_heap(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"empty heap create/destroy\";\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(NULL, 0);\n    CHECK(h != NULL, \"ua_create with no classes returned NULL\");\n    CHECK(ua_region_count(h) == 0, \"expected 0 regions, got %u\",\n          ua_region_count(h));\n    CHECK(ua_bytes_total(h) == 0, \"expected 0 total bytes\");\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_prewarming */\nstatic void test_prewarming(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"pre-warmed classes populate free-lists\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64,  .prewarm_count = 4 },\n        { .size = 256, .prewarm_count = 2 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 2);\n    CHECK(h != NULL, \"ua_create failed\");\n    CHECK(ua_region_count(h) == 1, \"expected 1 region, got %u\",\n          ua_region_count(h));\n    CHECK(ua_class_free_count(h, 64) == 4,\n          \"expected 4 free 64-byte chunks, got %u\",\n          ua_class_free_count(h, 64));\n    CHECK(ua_class_free_count(h, 256) == 2,\n          \"expected 2 free 256-byte chunks, got %u\",\n          ua_class_free_count(h, 256));\n    CHECK(ua_alloc_calls(h) == 0, \"no allocs yet\");\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_alloc_pop_from_free_list */\nstatic void test_alloc_pop_from_free_list(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"alloc pops from the matching class\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64, .prewarm_count = 2 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 1);\n    CHECK(h != NULL, \"create failed\");\n\n    ua_chunk_t *a = ua_alloc(h, 32);  /* fits in 64-byte class */\n    CHECK(a != NULL, \"alloc returned NULL\");\n    CHECK(ua_size(a) >= 32, \"chunk too small\");\n    CHECK(ua_class_free_count(h, 64) == 1, \"expected 1 left, got %u\",\n          ua_class_free_count(h, 64));\n\n    ua_chunk_t *b = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    CHECK(b != NULL, \"second alloc returned NULL\");\n    CHECK(ua_class_free_count(h, 64) == 0, \"expected 0 left\");\n    CHECK(ua_grow_calls(h) == 0, \"should not have grown\");\n\n    /* Distinct pointers — payloads don't overlap. */\n    CHECK(ua_data(a) != ua_data(b), \"two allocs gave same address\");\n\n    ua_unref(h, a);\n    ua_unref(h, b);\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_reuse_after_unref */\nstatic void test_reuse_after_unref(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"alloc reuses a freed chunk without growing\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 128, .prewarm_count = 1 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 1);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n\n    ua_chunk_t *a = ua_alloc(h, 128);\n    void       *pa = ua_data(a);\n    ua_unref(h, a);\n\n    ua_chunk_t *b = ua_alloc(h, 128);\n    CHECK(ua_data(b) == pa, \"expected same pointer back\");\n    CHECK(ua_grow_calls(h) == 0, \"should not have grown\");\n    ua_unref(h, b);\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_refcount_keeps_alive */\nstatic void test_refcount_keeps_alive(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"ua_ref prevents free on first unref\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64, .prewarm_count = 1 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 1);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n\n    ua_chunk_t *a = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    ua_ref(a);                 /* refcount = 2 */\n    ua_unref(h, a);            /* refcount = 1 — still live */\n\n    /* Trying to alloc again should NOT return `a`'s address — `a` is still\n     * in use. */\n    ua_chunk_t *b = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    CHECK(b != a, \"alloc reclaimed a chunk that still has a ref\");\n\n    ua_unref(h, a);            /* refcount = 0 — now freed */\n    ua_unref(h, b);\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_adjacent_merge */\nstatic void test_adjacent_merge(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"two adjacent frees merge into one larger chunk\";\n    /* Carve 3 chunks of size 64 contiguously in one region. */\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64, .prewarm_count = 3 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 1);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n\n    /* Take all three. Their payloads are in some order; we'll free\n     * them in physical-address order to trigger merging. */\n    ua_chunk_t *a = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    ua_chunk_t *b = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    ua_chunk_t *c = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    CHECK(a && b && c, \"alloc failed\");\n\n    /* Free a and b. They might or might not be physically adjacent\n     * depending on the order ua_alloc returns them. The free-list is\n     * LIFO, so the last-pushed is the first-popped. The test isn't\n     * sensitive to which physical order we got — we just need to\n     * verify that whichever two are adjacent merge when both freed. */\n    uint32_t merges_before = ua_neighbor_merges(h);\n    ua_unref(h, a);\n    ua_unref(h, b);\n    ua_unref(h, c);\n\n    /* After all three free, at minimum some neighbor merges should\n     * have happened (three physically adjacent chunks can fuse). */\n    CHECK(ua_neighbor_merges(h) > merges_before,\n          \"expected at least one neighbor merge, got %u\",\n          ua_neighbor_merges(h) - merges_before);\n\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_non_adjacent_stay_separate */\nstatic void test_non_adjacent_stay_separate(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"non-adjacent frees do not merge\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64, .prewarm_count = 3 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 1);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n\n    ua_chunk_t *a = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    ua_chunk_t *b = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    ua_chunk_t *c = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n\n    /* Free outermost; keep middle (whichever it is in physical\n     * order). We don't know which is middle in physical order from\n     * the API, so this test instead verifies a weaker but useful\n     * property: freeing only `a` produces no merges (the other two\n     * are still allocated, so no neighbors are free). */\n    uint32_t before = ua_neighbor_merges(h);\n    ua_unref(h, a);\n    CHECK(ua_neighbor_merges(h) == before,\n          \"expected no merges with neighbors allocated\");\n\n    ua_unref(h, b);\n    ua_unref(h, c);\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_fallthrough_to_larger_class */\nstatic void test_fallthrough_to_larger_class(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"alloc falls through to a larger class when small one is empty\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64,  .prewarm_count = 1 },\n        { .size = 256, .prewarm_count = 1 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 2);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n\n    /* Exhaust the 64 class. */\n    ua_chunk_t *a = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    CHECK(a, \"first alloc failed\");\n\n    /* Now ask for 64 again. Should NOT grow — should fall through\n     * to the 256 class. */\n    ua_chunk_t *b = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n    CHECK(b, \"second alloc failed\");\n    CHECK(ua_grow_calls(h) == 0, \"expected no growth, got %u\",\n          ua_grow_calls(h));\n    CHECK(ua_size(b) >= 64, \"chunk too small\");\n\n    ua_unref(h, a);\n    ua_unref(h, b);\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_growth_for_oversize */\nstatic void test_growth_for_oversize(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"alloc above all classes triggers growth\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64, .prewarm_count = 1 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 1);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n    CHECK(ua_region_count(h) == 1, \"should start with 1 region\");\n\n    ua_chunk_t *big = ua_alloc(h, 4096);\n    CHECK(big, \"oversized alloc returned NULL\");\n    CHECK(ua_size(big) >= 4096, \"chunk smaller than requested\");\n    CHECK(ua_region_count(h) == 2, \"expected 2 regions after growth, got %u\",\n          ua_region_count(h));\n    CHECK(ua_grow_calls(h) == 1, \"expected 1 grow call\");\n\n    ua_unref(h, big);\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_explicit_sweep_merges_runs */\nstatic void test_explicit_sweep_merges_runs(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"ua_sweep merges what eager merge couldn't catch\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64, .prewarm_count = 4 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 1);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n\n    ua_chunk_t *chunks[4];\n    for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) chunks[i] = ua_alloc(h, 64);\n\n    /* Free all four. Eager merge will catch most of this. The\n     * explicit sweep should be a no-op or near-no-op (since eager\n     * already did the work). The point of the test is that calling\n     * ua_sweep is safe and reports a sane count. */\n    for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) ua_unref(h, chunks[i]);\n\n    uint32_t sweep_count = ua_sweep(h);\n    CHECK(sweep_count == 0, \"expected 0 extra merges after eager already ran, got %u\",\n          sweep_count);\n\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_duplicate_size_sums_count */\nstatic void test_duplicate_size_sums_count(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"duplicate class sizes sum their prewarm counts\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64, .prewarm_count = 2 },\n        { .size = 64, .prewarm_count = 3 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 2);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n    CHECK(ua_class_free_count(h, 64) == 5,\n          \"expected 5 (2+3), got %u\", ua_class_free_count(h, 64));\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Concurrent stress */\ntypedef struct stress_arg {\n    ua_t    *h;\n    int      n_ops;\n    _Atomic uint32_t *write_failures;\n} stress_arg_t;\n\nstatic void *stress_worker(void *arg_)\n{\n    stress_arg_t *arg = arg_;\n    for (int i = 0; i < arg->n_ops; i++) {\n        size_t sz = 16 + (size_t)(rand() % 240);\n        ua_chunk_t *c = ua_alloc(arg->h, sz);\n        if (!c) {\n            atomic_fetch_add_explicit(arg->write_failures, 1u,\n                                      memory_order_relaxed);\n            continue;\n        }\n        /* Write the chunk to test no-overlap. */\n        memset(ua_data(c), 0xAB, ua_size(c));\n        /* Random hold pattern — sometimes ref-then-unref-twice. */\n        if (i % 3 == 0) {\n            ua_ref(c);\n            ua_unref(arg->h, c);\n        }\n        ua_unref(arg->h, c);\n    }\n    return NULL;\n}\n\nstatic void test_concurrent_stress(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"8-thread alloc/unref stress (1000 ops each)\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 64,  .prewarm_count = 32 },\n        { .size = 256, .prewarm_count = 16 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 2);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n\n    _Atomic uint32_t failures = 0;\n    pthread_t       threads[8];\n    stress_arg_t    args[8];\n    for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++) {\n        args[i].h              = h;\n        args[i].n_ops          = 1000;\n        args[i].write_failures = &failures;\n        pthread_create(&threads[i], NULL, stress_worker, &args[i]);\n    }\n    for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++) pthread_join(threads[i], NULL);\n\n    CHECK(failures == 0, \"saw %u alloc failures during stress\",\n          (uint32_t)failures);\n    CHECK(ua_bytes_in_use(h) == 0,\n          \"expected 0 bytes in use after stress, got %llu\",\n          (unsigned long long)ua_bytes_in_use(h));\n\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ test_ua_data_writes_persist */\nstatic void test_ua_data_writes_persist(void)\n{\n    const char *name = \"chunk payload survives across the API boundary\";\n    ua_class_decl_t classes[] = {\n        { .size = 256, .prewarm_count = 1 },\n    };\n    ua_t *h = ua_create(classes, 1);\n    CHECK(h, \"create failed\");\n\n    ua_chunk_t *c = ua_alloc(h, 200);\n    CHECK(c, \"alloc failed\");\n    uint8_t *d = ua_data(c);\n    for (int i = 0; i < 200; i++) d[i] = (uint8_t)(i & 0xFF);\n\n    /* Round-trip check: write a pattern, read it back. */\n    for (int i = 0; i < 200; i++) {\n        CHECK(d[i] == (uint8_t)(i & 0xFF), \"byte %d corrupted\", i);\n    }\n    ua_unref(h, c);\n    ua_destroy(h);\n    PASS(name);\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ main */\nint main(void)\n{\n    printf(\"016-unified-allocator-test:\\n\");\n    test_empty_heap();\n    test_prewarming();\n    test_alloc_pop_from_free_list();\n    test_reuse_after_unref();\n    test_refcount_keeps_alive();\n    test_adjacent_merge();\n    test_non_adjacent_stay_separate();\n    test_fallthrough_to_larger_class();\n    test_growth_for_oversize();\n    test_explicit_sweep_merges_runs();\n    test_duplicate_size_sums_count();\n    test_ua_data_writes_persist();\n    test_concurrent_stress();\n    printf(\"\\n  %d passed, %d failed\\n\", pass, fail);\n    return fail == 0 ? 0 : 1;\n}\n/* }}} */\n",
  "file_path":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/tests/016-unified-allocator-test.c"
}
